New `certify_catalog` `governance_actions` type (BOOK-24 §5 extension, additive — no new subsystem) gives the Provost a formal, audited annual Catalog Certification once a `CatalogEdition`'s dean→provost→registrar_publish workflow is complete — the durable artifact of record standing in for "represents the university before the Government Education System" (an honestly-scoped internal certification, not a fabricated external Ministry integration). Also fixed, found during KPI verification: `research` domain KPIs (SPRINT-21/NEW-33) were added to `KPI_REGISTRY` but never to `kpi_definitions`'s `VALID_KPI_DOMAINS`/DB check constraint, 500ing `publish_all_for_tenant` for every tenant on the first research KPI it reached. **Recording-prep verification pass (2026-08-07, same day) found and fixed 5 more real, pre-existing bugs while actually driving the 11-step demo end-to-end**: `ilo_coverage_pct` had no real consumer on Provost Command itself (added an "Institution KPIs" section to `RectorBridge.js`, correcting its own stale header comment); `ProgramGovernancePanel.js` could never reload an already-submitted catalog workflow on a fresh page load (new `GET .../programs/{id}/catalog-workflow`); `course_access.py`'s dean/provost course visibility silently returned zero courses always (`platform.role_scopes` has no writer anywhere — now derived structurally from `College.dean_user_id`/`Institution.tenant_id`); `GET /api/admin/users` is registered TWICE (`admin.py` + `admin_enhanced.py`, each with an independent role allowlist missing `provost` — fixed both, `admin.py`'s copy is the one actually served); and, most severe, NEITHER the PLO→ILO nor CLO→PLO alignment grid had EVER persisted an alignment for ANY persona (`institutionAPI.js` sent `plo_id`/`ilo_id` friendly names, `programs.py`'s schema expects generic `source_id`/`target_id` — silent 422 on every attempt); plus `POST/PATCH /api/institution/terms` 500'd on every call (tz-aware ISO input into a `TIMESTAMP WITHOUT TIME ZONE` column), so "define the Academic Calendar" had never worked through the real UI either. NEW-17's Tier-1 pre-evaluation limiter was reviewed for coupling to `RateLimitService` and deliberately left self-contained — its business-data-derived rolling-window count has no drift risk, and genuine coupling would change actual block semantics (a product policy call, not a code gap). The real finding: the legacy audit trail was silently near-completely broken — nearly every `AuditService.log_action` call site never awaited it (an `async def`, silently a no-op), `log_action`'s own internal `execute()`/`commit()` calls weren't awaited either (no-op with `AsyncSession`, used by `auth.py`'s OIDC routes), `AuditLoggingMiddleware` had a permanent `lambda: None` session-factory placeholder since it was added, and — found only by testing against a real disposable Postgres — the table was missing `endpoint`/`http_method` columns in every definition (raw SQL bootstrap scripts and the ORM model) and `log_action` hardcoded the wrong Postgres metadata column name (`meta_data` instead of the real `metadata`, misreading an ORM attribute alias). Fixed all of it, adopted `platform.audit_logs` into the Alembic chain (same legacy-script-only status as `legal_holds`/`tenant_settings`/`rate_limits`), added a nightly partition-maintenance Celery beat job (the table's Postgres-native monthly partitioning had no partitions past March 2026), and mounted NEW-15's hash-chained audit fabric REST API (`backend/api/routes/audit.py`, fully built and tested but never mounted) — with `require_admin()` added to every route first, since it had none and would otherwise have been a new cross-tenant vulnerability (verified live: unauthenticated → 401, non-admin → 403). Two other researched sub-items (BOOK-09 Ch. 7 per-move calibration + steward alerts; BOOK-10 Ch. 8 misbehaviour escalation ladder) remain open, both converging on the same missing piece — a real steward role + review surface — documented but not built this pass. See `docs/sprint_decisions_20260809_agent_governance_budget_hardening.md`)** · **Driver/integration + data/model hardening — small/mechanical tier ✅ (2026-08-10, no new G-register item)**: the user's own next-priority pick, small/mechanical tier only (LTI full JWT verification, analytics real SQL, Moodle grade-passback ID-mapping, marketing domain, GPS ESCO crosswalk, G7 schema change, IW4-6 all explicitly deferred). `gateway_config_sync.delete_provider` was a no-op (`logger.warning(...); return True`) — now calls the real `POST /model/delete` keyed by `provider_id` (matching `_to_gateway_model`'s own `model_info.id = str(provider.id)` at registration), wired into `llm_provider_service.soft_delete_provider` post-commit, fail-open on gateway error. `ai_management`'s Test-Skill preview (`test_skill`) always returned a literal MVP-stub string — now a real LLM invocation mirroring `test_agent`'s pattern (`{{key}}` substitution into `skill.user_prompt_tpl`, new `service_type="skill_test_preview"` attribution row), kept this route's own error-via-response-field convention rather than raising. `graduation.predicted` premise-corrected: `student_success_consumers.py`'s own docstring claimed no producer existed — already false when written, since `gps_worker.recompute_path_health_task` has emitted it since STX-07's first commit; the real bugs were the emission gate (`has_adopted_path` alone fired on nearly every recompute, predicting nothing — tightened to `time_to_graduation.max_terms <= 1`) and the one registered consumer being a dormant `logger.info`-only stub — now enqueues a new `refresh_career_gap_analysis_task` (re-runs `career_gap_service.analyze_gap` per active `TwinCareerGoal`, deliberately no new persistence layer, same compute-on-read posture as its own on-demand route). This same STX-14/15 row's own "no producer exists" claim above corrected in place (this repo's convention for live/current-state docs); `sprint_decisions_20260724_stx14_15.md` itself left untouched as the historical record. A critical, unrelated bug found first and fixed separately while researching LTI: `lti_oidc_init` had a live, unauthenticated open redirect (`target_link_uri` redirected to with zero validation, no `lti_platform` table existing yet to check `iss` against) — fixed with a same-origin/bare-path allow-list rejecting protocol-relative and backslash-bypass tricks, committed separately (`0d6bebc`). A live decoy found during research and separately confirmed for removal: `/advisor/dashboard` rendered hardcoded fake KPIs and two fabricated named students ("Bob Martinez", "Carol Davis") unconditionally, as if real — replaced with the same honest not-yet-available `Card` idiom `AlumniDashboard.js`/`StudentDashboard.js` already use; the real gap (no advisor-assignment relationship exists anywhere in this data model) is not fixed by this pass, only honestly disclosed. See `docs/sprint_decisions_20260810_driver_integration_hardening.md`)** · **Driver/integration hardening II — small/contained tier from the moderate/large backlog ✅ (2026-08-11, no new G-register item)**: continuation of the pass above — seven parallel research agents re-surveyed the deferred moderate/large backlog and found several premises wrong once verified: analytics revenue/quality metrics is genuinely LARGE (3 of 4 KPI calc-types hardcoded to return zero, no scheduler exists), not moderate as first labeled; Moodle grade-passback is smaller than "large" (real driver + real ID-mapping tables already existed, just unpopulated); the marketing domain (`journeys.py`/`crm.py`) isn't a sizing problem at all — its routers were never mounted in `main.py`, so the real, routed frontend pages have 404'd since they were built (flagged for removal per the user's own choice, nothing implemented). GPS `best_career_path` premise-corrected and wired onto the existing 8-occupation ESCO-lite seed (`career_gap_service._skill_has_competency` reused, not duplicated; course selection itself is not re-optimized) — `lowest_cost` stays an honest gap with its stale "STX-12 brings them" reason replaced by the real blocker (no monetary field exists anywhere in the schema). New IW5 Steward Console (`GET /api/institution/steward-console`, separate route file from IW1-3 for a narrower role gate matching `CrisisIncident`'s own restricted-visibility posture) composes already-real `AgentGateRun`/`CrisisIncident`/`CalibrationBaseline` data into agent scorecards, release-gate history, an incident queue, and an honest calibration-drift gap — registered in `main.py`'s two-step router mount (import + allow-list), the exact trap NEW-05 already found once for `academic_gps`/`recognition`. New Moodle `assessment.completed` → grade-passback consumer on the `n8n-bridge` group (mirroring `esse3_credential_consumers.py`), pushing to Moodle's course-total grade slot — a deliberate v1 narrowing, since `MoodleGradeMapping`'s per-activity mapping has zero writers anywhere in this codebase. A test-infrastructure side-quest found and fixed four independent, pre-existing bugs blocking a real-Postgres container-to-container test run (never exercised this way before): root `conftest.py` unconditionally clobbered `DLU_TEST_PG_URL`; a raw-SQL bootstrap typo'd `platform.tenants`' `metadata` column as `meta_data`; a dead, duplicate, SQLite-only DDL block for `platform.saml_configurations` shadowed the ORM's own correct table; and `docker-compose.test.yml`'s test Postgres image lacked the pgvector extension `CLO.embedding` needs. G7 regulation-year and LTI full JWT verification were re-verified and confirmed at their original size, both still deferred. See `docs/sprint_decisions_20260811_driver_integration_hardening_ii.md`)** · **Category 2 hardening — governed per-institution policy calibration infrastructure ✅ (2026-08-12, no new G-register item)**: continues the user's own stated priority order (code gaps, then ops/business gaps, then infrastructure) into category 2 for the first time. A fresh re-verification confirmed almost all of category 2 genuinely cannot be substituted for by code (external audit, legal sign-off, agent GA gating, live third-party credentials, real production PITR all remain exactly as open as before) — but surfaced one real, code-shaped gap hiding inside it: per-institution policy calibration was a MIXED bag, not a uniform gap. `ItemCalibrationParams`/`CompetencyEngineParams` already had a real, governed, versioned, institution-scoped-with-global-fallback override mechanism (just never populated per-institution); `move_policy_service.RISK_THRESHOLD`, `recommendation_service_v2`'s rank weights + `SERENDIPITY_QUOTA`, and `consolidation_service`'s salience weights had NO override mechanism at all — plain hardcoded Python module constants. New `RiskPolicyParams`/`RecommendationPolicyParams`/`SalienceWeightParams` (one migration, same immutable-row shape as `ItemCalibrationParams`) close that gap — the knob, not the calibration decision itself, which remains a genuine steward/institution-authority call, unmade for any institution before or after this pass. `build_assessment` resolving the new `RiskPolicyParams` rippled into 8 existing test files (every agent's `run_cycle`/`run_mission` passes through it) — found running the full suite, not assumed, matching the exact ripple-discovery discipline the agent-governance hardening pass's own `NotificationPreference` fix established. See `docs/sprint_decisions_20260812_category2_policy_calibration_hardening.md`)** · **Large-tier driver/integration backlog — all 6 deferred items closed ✅ (2026-08-15, `dlu_builder_tk`, no new G-register item)**: closes the moderate/large-tier backlog deferred by the two prior driver/integration hardening passes — LTI 1.3 full JWT verification, GPS `lowest_cost`, G7 regulation-year schema binding, Institution Workspace IW4 (Advisor Workspace) + IW6 (Operator Plane), and a full ESCO occupation crosswalk — planned as one 6-item effort and executed/committed one item at a time in dependency order. G7: `program_courses.program_version_id` (nullable FK, two PARTIAL unique indexes — Postgres treats multiple NULLs as distinct, so a naive 3-column unique would have silently allowed duplicates for the version-agnostic case every existing row is in) makes NEW-04's regulation-year fingerprint tag actually change computed plans instead of being cosmetic-only; no backfill to a specific version (an honest NULL default) to avoid silently emptying a different cohort's requirement set. GPS `lowest_cost`: new `cost_per_credit` column, honesty-gated total (any course missing cost/credits data keeps the whole scenario `not_yet_available`). LTI: new `LtiPlatform` registration table + real JWKS-fetch-and-cryptographically-verify path via `PyJWKClient` (no genuine verified-JWT pattern existed anywhere in this codebase before this — the closest analog, `oidc_service.validate_id_token`, is confirmed unverified) — OIDC init now redirects to a registered platform's real auth endpoint when one resolves, falling back to the prior demo behavior unchanged otherwise. IW4: new `AdvisorAssignment` table (partial-unique-indexed for soft-delete/reassignment) + management-gated CRUD + an own-caseload-default composite (GPS hedges, risk stream); also fixed a real latent bug in `move_proposal_service.list_for_twin` (reached into a client-specific private `_cache` dict instead of the real `redis.asyncio.Redis` `scan_iter` interface every other cache-backed service already uses — verified empirically that this doesn't currently manifest in production, since `backend.core.cache.redis_client` is still always the same in-process mock everywhere, but would silently break the moment a real Redis client is ever substituted). IW6: driver-health snapshot reusing the three existing per-driver `CircuitBreaker` singletons directly (already process-wide, never needed per-tenant scoping), a new read-only `ConsumerGroup.mesh_lag()` sibling to `drain()` (zero `XREADGROUP`/`XAUTOCLAIM`/handler dispatch — proven with a fake Redis object that lacks those methods entirely), and one new anonymized cross-tenant AI-spend aggregate — gated to `platform_admin`/`super_admin` only, no new role invented. ESCO: sourced REAL data from the official public ESCO REST API (confirmed reachable — the plan's own honest "if not fetchable" fallback did not apply) — 2,700 of ESCO's ~2,942 published occupations (91.8%), 104,504 real skill-relation rows, via a pagination quirk found and fixed empirically (ESCO's own `offset` param is a page index, not an item-skip count); replaces the STX-14/15 hand-curated 8-occupation `urn:dlu-esco-lite:` seed via the first CSV-bundled bulk-insert migration in this codebase; `career_gap_service._skill_has_competency`'s matching logic deliberately unchanged (verified via a direct sanity break-and-revert check); every live "ESCO-lite" doc reference across `backend/` swept and corrected, including the eval scenario bank and the Career Advisor agent's own persisted system prompt. All 6 migrations verified end-to-end against a disposable Postgres (clean upgrade/downgrade/re-upgrade round-trip, exact data landed each time); 51 new tests; `phase1` gate 104 passed/3 skipped/0 failed unchanged throughout; a full raw-suite run's 68 failures confirmed pre-existing and unrelated (zero file overlap with anything touched, spot-checked across 5 failure categories — live-infrastructure dependencies this environment doesn't have, or pre-existing test/code drift entirely outside this effort). See `docs/sprint_decisions_20260815_large_tier_backlog_summary.md` (cross-references all 6 per-item docs)** · **Category 3 infrastructure gaps — all 3 items closed ✅ (2026-08-19, `dlu_builder_tk`, no new G-register item)**: closes the user's own stated priority order's last tier (`docs/sprint_decisions_20260807_security_tenancy_hardening.md` lines 3-5/159-161: "no real deployment target/IaC, alerting not reaching anyone, no automated backup schedule"). **Alerting** (2026-08-16): Prometheus's `alerting:` block, commented out since inception, now points at a new Alertmanager instance (staging + production compose); a new in-app webhook receiver (`POST /api/internal/alerts/webhook`, mirrors `internal.py`'s shared-secret auth pattern) always logs every received alert and optionally forwards to a configured Slack/webhook URL — degrading to log-only honestly, never claiming fake delivery. Found while closing this: most pre-existing alert rules (all of the orphaned `prometheus-alerts-pi4.yml`, a real subset of the already-mounted `alerts.yml`) reference metrics that are never incremented anywhere or don't exist at all — confirmed by tracing real `.inc()`/`.set()`/`.observe()` call sites, not assumed from the rule file alone; only the 2 confirmed-live orphaned rule files (event-mesh, eval-harness) are wired in, `pi4`'s stays deliberately unmounted rather than faking working payment/workflow/AI-governance/tenant-isolation alerting that can never fire. **Backup automation** (2026-08-17): a dedicated Celery beat+worker pair runs `pg_dump` nightly with `scripts/backup_production.sh`'s own 7-day retention policy; `postgresql-client` added to `backend/Dockerfile` so `pg_dump` is actually present in the worker container. Found while closing this: repo-wide search confirmed `celery beat` has never run in ANY environment (dev/turnkey/italian run a plain worker only; staging/production run no Celery process at all) — all ~20 pre-existing `beat_schedule` entries have therefore never fired anywhere, ever. This is a much bigger finding than backup automation alone — turning it on wholesale would activate 20 previously-dormant production tasks (behaviour recompute, GPS recompute, ESSE3/Frappe sync, event-mesh drain, etc.) at once, a real blast-radius decision — surfaced to the user directly rather than decided unilaterally; the user chose a minimal, dedicated beat+worker pair scoped to backup only (`task_default_queue="backup"`, combined `-B` process, single-replica), leaving the shared scheduler's ~20 other entries exactly as dormant as before, disclosed as pre-existing debt rather than fixed. **Deployment/IaC** (2026-08-18): new CI workflow builds and pushes `backend`/`frontend` Dockerfiles to GitHub Container Registry using the repo's own `GITHUB_TOKEN` — closes the loop `docker-compose.production.yml` already assumed existed (`${PRODUCTION_BACKEND_IMAGE}`/`${PRODUCTION_FRONTEND_IMAGE}`, confirmed previously referencing images nothing ever built via a direct `docker compose --profile ops config` failure); that same compose file's image references now default to the exact GHCR path this workflow pushes to, so `docker compose config` resolves cleanly with zero env vars supplied. Deliberately no Kubernetes/Terraform — BOOK-18 Ch. 6 already treats orchestrator choice as an ops decision, not an architecture one this repo needs to make. 15 new tests across the two code-bearing items; every new/changed infra file verified with its real authoritative tool (`promtool check config`, `amtool check-config`, `docker compose config --quiet`, `actionlint`), not just visual inspection; full raw-suite regression (2,782 passed, 52 failed, 5 errors) confirmed the same pre-existing, unrelated failure categories already documented one day earlier in the large-tier-backlog summary above (68 failed/5 errors — SAML, Kong, PI3/Stripe, RAG, KG ontology lint, load-test concurrency), zero file overlap with anything touched. See `docs/sprint_decisions_20260819_category3_summary.md` (cross-references all 3 per-item docs)** · **Investor-demo readiness — all 4 items delivered ✅ (2026-08-20, `dlu_builder_tk`, no new G-register item — a demo/pilot-readiness pass, not a regulatory gap)**: the platform's headline feature — the nine named agents seeded since STX-14/15 — had never been seeded outside the test suite, so a fresh environment showed an empty agent registry and no Twin/GPS/credential/recognition data; new `--scenario=ai_native_demo` (`backend/scripts/seed_demo.py`) seeds all 9 agents, 5 Student Digital Twins, a real GPS `fastest_path` scenario, a signed credential, a granted recognition claim, a Tier-1 pre-evaluation, viva evidence, a real advisor caseload, and one demo login per persona (student/instructor/admin/platform_admin) — reusing real service functions throughout, never hand-written rows bypassing business logic; found and worked around two pre-existing `academic_gps_service.py` bugs along the way (`platform.course_catalog_items` has no creation migration anywhere; a `not_yet_available` reason string exceeds `PathScenario.reason`'s own 200-char column limit, aborting the 4-scenario batch insert — both disclosed, neither fixed, out of this pass's scope). Separately found the AI-native student pages were completely unreachable from the app's own navigation: the component first assumed to be the live nav (`Sidebar.js`) turned out to be dead code with zero imports anywhere in the frontend, and the actual live one (`PaperNav.js`) showed the identical course-authoring toolbar to every logged-in persona regardless of role — including a `platform_admin` with literally no click-path to Operator Plane at all (nested under an `admin`-only dropdown, a different role set than `operator_plane.py`'s own `_require_operator`) despite being fully authorized server-side. Fixed: persona-aware post-login redirect (a plain student/operator now lands on their own real page, not the generic `/dashboard`), the course-authoring toolbar hidden for a "pure" student or operator account (any overlapping staff role still sees it), and Operator Plane promoted to its own persona-gated top-level link. Also built the two institution-workspace pages (IW4 Advisor Workspace, IW6 Operator Plane) the large-tier backlog above had left backend-only with no UI at all. All four personas (Student/Teacher/Admin/Operator) rehearsed and screen-recorded end to end against a live local instance, one browser-session recording per login; `docs/INVESTOR_DEMO_WALKTHROUGH.md` is the resulting script. Found, disclosed, deliberately not fixed here (flagged for a future pass): the entire `backend/domains/payments/` subsystem (financial holds, invoices, installments) has no Alembic migration anywhere — surfaces as a real 500 on the Credential Wallet's clearance-checklist section, degrading to an honest empty state rather than crashing the page | 18/19/20 | **DAS-Certified auditable** (external audit — remains the one item this Annex update cannot itself close) |

| **EKG-W0-01 — Postgres Ontology Registry** (`dlu_builder_tk`, ✅ **implemented**): 4 `platform`-schema tables (`ekg_ontology_versions`/`ekg_node_types`/`ekg_edge_types`/`ekg_shacl_shapes`, migration `20260919_0900_ekg_w0_01_ontology_registry.py`) + `ekg_ontology_registry_service.py` (CRUD, `get_current_version` resolver, immutable-once-approved via SQLAlchemy `before_update`/`before_delete` listeners — `ItemCalibrationParams` pattern) + `seed_ekg_ontology_v1.py`, seeding the **real, honest count**: 37 node types / 49 edge types (not the rounded "~35/~40" used in prose elsewhere — corrected here and in `dlu-core.yaml`'s own comment) / 3 SHACL shapes, published as registry v1.0.0. Verified against real migrated Postgres: upgrade→downgrade→upgrade clean; 13/13 conformance tests pass (`tests/conformance/test_ekg_ontology_registry.py`) — seeding, idempotency, immutability (version + child-row mutation/delete/insert all rejected once approved), lookup by layer/key, current-version resolution across multiple published versions | 05 | EKG-W0-01 | 13 tests: seed count assertions, idempotent re-seed, 5 immutability-rejection tests, 4 lookup tests, 2 current-version-resolution tests |
| **EKG-W0-02 — Neo4j bootstrap & tenancy policy** (`dlu_builder_tk`, ✅ **implemented, with one real design correction**): `backend/scripts/ekg_neo4j_bootstrap.py` reads the CURRENT approved Postgres registry (EKG-W0-01) and generates/applies Neo4j constraints+indexes for whatever node types it contains — never a second static list to hand-maintain. **Found live against the dev stack**: this Neo4j is Community Edition (`neo4j:5-community`), which rejects composite `(tenantId, id) IS NODE KEY` constraints (Enterprise-only) — 35/37 constraint statements failed on first run. Corrected to a single-property `id IS UNIQUE` constraint on every label (safe: EKG ids are stable UUIDs by the ontology's own identity policy, unlike the legacy KG's per-tenant-schema Integer `course_id`, which genuinely needed a composite key) plus a plain `tenantId` index on TENANT-scoped labels for query performance. `ekg_scope_policy.py` classifies node labels GLOBAL (`FrameworkConcept`, `QualificationLevel` only — external, standards-referenced, pinned-by-version) vs TENANT (the other 35, conservative default). `backend/services/graph/named_query_templates.py` is the ONE sanctioned entry point to the graph (label-identifier validation against Cypher injection; every TENANT-scoped op requires a resolved `TenantContext`, every GLOBAL op rejects one) — this is where isolation is actually enforced, independent of the DB constraint. 20/20 conformance tests pass across both EKG suites (`test_ekg_ontology_registry.py` + `test_ekg_neo4j_bootstrap_and_templates.py`), including a real two-tenant cross-read rejection test against live Neo4j; bootstrap verified idempotent (`SHOW CONSTRAINTS`/`SHOW INDEXES` re-run clean); both Postgres and Neo4j confirmed left with zero stray state after the suite | 05, 19 | EKG-W0-02 | 7 tests: constraint/index generation shape, live apply + idempotent re-apply (`SHOW CONSTRAINTS`/`SHOW INDEXES`), tenant-scope policy rejection (both directions), malformed-label rejection, live two-tenant isolation (cross-tenant read returns null, list/count never leak) |

| **EKG-W1-03 — Knowledge projection (Concept + PREREQUISITE_OF/RELATED_TO/PART_OF)** (`dlu_builder_tk`, ✅ **implemented, scoped down honestly, plus a pre-existing broken-code finding fixed as a genuine prerequisite — TEACHES/ADDRESSES/HAS_CONCEPT disclosed as carry-forwards**): live investigation found NO Postgres `Concept`/`KnowledgeUnit` model exists anywhere (confirmed via `grep -rn "^class Concept\b"`/`"^class KnowledgeUnit\b"` across the whole backend — zero matches), a materially different starting point than W1-01/W1-02, which both had real Postgres source rows already. THREE separate, pre-existing "concept-like" systems were found instead: (1) the Neo4j-only "legacy KG v1.0" (`:Concept {course_id, tenant_id, name}`, `PREREQUISITE_FOR`/`SAME_AS` edges, `kg_build_service.py`) — not Postgres-sourced, out of scope; (2) a course-level `CoursePrerequisite`/`curriculum_graph_service.py` DAG guard — a different entity (courses, not concepts) whose DFS-cycle-check STYLE this sprint's own guard reuses; (3) a pre-existing, course-scoped, GENERIC "Wave 3 Knowledge Graph" (`KnowledgeGraphNode`/`KnowledgeGraphEdge`, `models.py`) with real CRUD routes and a `node_type` enum that includes `CONCEPT`, and an edge-type enum that includes `RELATED_TO`/`PREREQUISITE`/`PART_OF` — the closest, most defensible EKG Concept source, and the one this sprint wires. **A genuine, load-bearing discovery along the way**: `KnowledgeGraphEdge` has no `label` column (confirmed via `ast.parse` against the model AND a live constructor call raising `TypeError`), yet BOTH of that system's edge-creation call sites (`add_knowledge_graph_edge`, the manual endpoint, AND `add_nodes_and_edges_to_graph`, the bulk-extraction path used by `extract_and_build_knowledge_graph`) passed `label=` anyway — meaning the entire edge-ingest half of this pre-existing, unrelated feature has never worked in production, not merely been untested. Fixed as a genuine prerequisite (not scope creep): a "cycle-detection guard on ingest" is meaningless on an ingest path that crashes before the invariant would ever matter. Real scope executed: new `backend/services/graph/knowledge_projection.py` maps ONLY `node_type == CONCEPT` rows onto the EKG `Concept` node type (`Topic`/`Skill`/`Person`/`Organization`/`Event`/`Location`/`Term` have no unambiguous EKG analog at this layer — `Skill` in particular is a tempting but wrong mapping, since EKG's own `Skill` is a curated Competency-layer taxonomy entity, not a free-text content mention) and ONLY `RELATED_TO`/`PREREQUISITE`→`PREREQUISITE_OF`/`PART_OF` edges between two Concept-typed endpoints (`EXAMPLE_OF`/`SIMILAR_TO`/`DEPENDS_ON`/`EXTENDS` have no EKG counterpart). `KnowledgeUnit` is never emitted — no source system has an analog — so `HAS_CONCEPT` is a disclosed gap. New `_detect_prerequisite_cycle` (DFS, mirroring `curriculum_graph_service.validate_prerequisite_chain`'s own pattern applied to `KnowledgeGraphEdge`) rejects a cyclic PREREQUISITE outright in the manual single-edge endpoint (`PrerequisiteCycleError`, surfaced by the route as HTTP 409 with the offending node-id path) and skips-and-reports (not aborts) in the bulk-extraction path, since one bad edge in an LLM-extracted batch shouldn't fail the whole batch. Two new events, `CONCEPT_CHANGED` (node) and `CONCEPT_RELATION_CHANGED` (edge), wired into all three of that system's write paths in the same transaction as the business write — via `event_outbox.emit_sync`, not `emit_async`: this whole producer surface runs on SYNC `Session`s (`get_sync_db`), the first EKG sprint to do so, which also means the producer and the `ekg-projection` consumer share the SAME session style for the first time — no async/sync boundary to cross in this sprint's own end-to-end test, unlike W1-01/W1-02. `TEACHES` (Lesson→Concept) and `ADDRESSES` (Lesson→MLO) remain disclosed carry-forwards: still blocked by the same missing-Lesson-node gap W1-02 already named (EKG-W1-06 territory), now also confirmed unregressed. 18/18 new tests pass: bug-fix regression (confirms the `label` `TypeError` was real, then confirms it's gone, plus a negative control proving the test isn't vacuous), 5 cycle-detection unit tests (no-cycle/direct/transitive/unrelated/safe-forward-chain) against a fake session, a real-Postgres integration test building an actual A→B→C prerequisite chain and confirming both the manual endpoint's rejection (with the exact expected cycle path) AND the bulk path's skip-and-report behavior, static-scan tests (no direct Neo4j call in any of the 3 producer methods; positive outbox-emission checks; the route's 409 handling), and 1 real end-to-end test (two real Concept nodes + a real PREREQUISITE edge created through the actual service methods, projected via the real `project_node_event`/`project_edge_event`, verified landing in real Neo4j with the `PREREQUISITE_OF` edge intact). Found and fixed one more real cross-test isolation gap of this sprint's own making, the same class W1-02 already named but a new trigger: this sprint's own end-to-end test calls `asyncio.run(...)` to provision a throwaway Ontology Registry version via the async registry service, and — like W1-02's `setup_institution` background-task finding — left the async engine's pooled connection bound to that call's own now-dead event loop; fixed by disposing the pool immediately after provisioning, before any sync work in the test resumes. 91/91 tests pass across all seven EKG sprints (W0-01…04 + W1-01/02/03) run together, repeatedly, with zero stray state confirmed in both Postgres and Neo4j after each run | 04, 05, 13 | EKG-W1-03 | 18 tests: 4 bug-fix regression, 5 cycle-detection unit tests, 7 static-scan tests (3× no-direct-graph-write, 3× positive outbox-emission, 1 route-409 check), 1 real-Postgres cycle-rejection + bulk-skip integration test, 1 real end-to-end producer→consumer→Neo4j test |
| **EKG-W1-04 — Assessment projection (Assessment/Rubric/RubricCriterion/PerformanceLevel)** (`dlu_builder_tk`, ✅ **implemented against a real, well-formed but UNMOUNTED module — a bigger reality-mismatch than any prior sprint, escalated to the user before proceeding, not decided unilaterally**): investigation found `backend/domains/assessment/` (`Assessment`/`Rubric`/`RubricCriterion`/`RubricLevel` models, `AssessmentService`, `RubricEngine`) maps closely to the EKG Ontology's own Assessment-layer types — but its ~1300-line route file is **never mounted** in `main.py` (no `_import_router("assessment", ...)` call, absent from both router allow-lists, not imported by the central models package) — the entire REST surface is dead, unreachable code, confirmed via exhaustive grep, not assumed. `AssessmentItem`/`PerformanceLevel` (exact ontology names) exist under no matching model; `RubricLevel` is a naming-only match for `PerformanceLevel`. Presented the user four options (wire the dead module without mounting it / mount the router this sprint / project off the live-but-loosely-typed `OutcomeAssessmentAlignment` instead / full carry-forward) — **user chose "wire the dead module, don't mount it,"** since mounting ~1300 lines of never-exercised route code is a separate, riskier product decision this sprint doesn't make unilaterally. Real scope executed: new `backend/services/graph/assessment_projection.py` builds `ASSESSMENT_DEFINITION_CHANGED`/`ASSESSMENT_RUBRIC_CHANGED`/`ASSESSMENT_CRITERION_CHANGED`/`ASSESSMENT_PERFORMANCE_LEVEL_CHANGED` node payloads (4 new suffixes under the already-registered `assessment` noun — deliberately NOT reusing the pre-existing `ASSESSMENT_COMPLETED`/`ASSESSMENT_SESSION_PUBLISHED`/`_CHANGED`, a real, checked collision found before shipping: those three are about the unrelated `AssessmentSession` exam-sitting aggregate and per-attempt grading results, with payload shapes incompatible with node projection — same class of finding as `COURSE_ATTRIBUTES_CHANGED`, EKG-W1-02); wired into `AssessmentService.create_assessment`/`update_assessment` and `RubricEngine.create_rubric`/`update_rubric`/`add_criterion`/`update_criterion`/`add_level`/`update_level` (same transaction as each write, `event_outbox.emit_sync`, same sync-session style as EKG-W1-03). `USES_RUBRIC`/`HAS_CRITERION`/`HAS_PERFORMANCE_LEVEL` all embed via real FKs (`Assessment.rubric_id`, `RubricCriterion.rubric_id`, `RubricLevel.criterion_id`) available at each row's own creation time — the same "one event, one node + its structural edge" pattern as EKG-W1-02/03. Disclosed as blocked, with no live OR even within-module mutation point to hang a producer on: `AssessmentItem` (no model anywhere), `HAS_ITEM`/`ASSESSES` (both need `AssessmentItem`), `ASSESSED_BY` (would need `CLOAlignment`, a model that exists with the right FK shape but has ZERO producers anywhere in the codebase — confirmed via grep — not even inside this same dead module). Rubric decomposition invariant (task 2, "criterion→levels ≥2") was genuinely unenforced before this sprint — `rubric_engine.submit_for_approval`'s own pre-existing gate only required `>= 1` level; tightened to match EKG-Ontology-1.0.md §5's own registered `HAS_PERFORMANCE_LEVEL` cardinality ("2..*", never previously enforced anywhere), extracted into a directly-testable `_find_underdecomposed_criteria` helper (mirroring EKG-W1-03's own cycle-guard extraction) — required updating ONE pre-existing mock-based test (`test_submit_for_approval`) whose fixture asserted the OLD, insufficiently-strict behavior, plus a new negative test for the tightened threshold. "Every published outcome is assessed-or-waived" (task 2's other invariant) was found to have **no "waived" concept anywhere in this codebase** for outcome/assessment coverage (14 files matched "waive/waiver" — faculty qualification, AVA accreditation Dtot/Ttot, credit-recognition policy, document/mobility/AP-invoicing waivers — none reusable here) and no CLO/MLO status field to define "published" directly either (defined transitively via `Course.workflow_state`/legacy `status`) — inventing a real waiver mechanism is a new academic-governance FEATURE, not EKG plumbing, so this sprint delivers new `backend/scripts/ekg_outcome_coverage_report.py` instead: an honest AUDIT REPORT (not a hard gate) flagging published-course CLOs with zero recorded coverage, reading the LIVE, mounted `OutcomeAssessmentAlignment` table (not the dead module's own `CLOAlignment`, which would report every CLO uncovered regardless of reality) — a deliberate, disclosed split between which system feeds the graph projection (ontology-aligned but unmounted) versus which feeds the coverage audit (live but loosely-typed). **Two more real, load-bearing bugs found and fixed while testing against real Postgres (both invisible to this module's own pre-existing mock-only test suite)**: (1) `platform.rubrics`/`assessments`/`rubric_criteria`/`rubric_levels` already existed in the shared test DB as a STALE, long-abandoned schema (varchar(36) ids, missing `total_points` column, a `check_rubric_status` constraint missing `'active'`) that predates the current model definitions and was never migrated forward — confirmed empty (0 rows) before being dropped and recreated from the current model metadata, test-locally; (2) all 13 `SQLEnum(..., native_enum=False)` columns across the module (`Assessment.status`/`.assessment_type`/`.grading_method`/`.bloom_level`, `Submission.status`, `Rubric.status`, `RubricCriterion.bloom_level`, `AssessmentQA`/`PlagiarismReport`/`ModerationRequest` statuses) serialized to the enum member's `.name` (e.g. `"DRAFT"`) by SQLAlchemy's own default for `native_enum=False`, while every matching `CheckConstraint` expects the lowercase `.value` (e.g. `'draft'`) — meaning `create_rubric`/`create_assessment` and every other status-setting call in this module would raise `CheckViolation` against a real database, a defect FULLY invisible to the mock-based suite; fixed uniformly across all 13 columns (`values_callable=lambda x: [e.value for e in x]`), the same "fix it everywhere, not just where my own test happened to hit it" discipline as EKG-W1-03's `label=` bug. Also found, a second time in as many sprints: `platform.outcome_assessment_alignments` (the LIVE system's own table) has no migration anywhere in the repo either — only the model class and a later migration's prose comment assuming it already existed; created test-locally via `checkfirst=True`, same as the schema-repair above. | **EKG-W1-05 — Accreditation trace queries** (`dlu_builder_tk`, ✅ **implemented against a materially different reality than the sprint's own named chain — investigated at the ONTOLOGY level, not just the producer level, before writing any query; scope decision escalated to the user, not decided unilaterally**): the sprint's task list names a "Program→CLO→MLO→Lesson→Assessment→Evidence trace" — checked against `seed_ekg_ontology_v1.py`'s own 49 registered edge types and found it doesn't correspond to a real Cypher path even in principle, independent of what's live: `CONTRIBUTES_TO` is `MLO -> CLO` (not "CLO→MLO"), `ADDRESSES` is `Lesson -> MLO` (not "MLO→Lesson") — both reversed from the spec's implied direction; **no edge type connects `Lesson` to `Assessment` at all**, in either direction, despite a real `Assessment.lesson_id` Postgres column that the ontology never modeled as a graph edge; **no edge type connects `Assessment` to `Evidence` at all** — `Evidence` only reaches `LearningOutcome` (`EVIDENCES`) and `RubricCriterion` (`EVALUATED_WITH`); and `ASSESSED_BY` (`LearningOutcome -> Assessment`) targets the literal Neo4j label `LearningOutcome`, which only ILO/PLO rows ever carry (EKG-W1-01's own `_LEVELS_UNDER_LEARNING_OUTCOME` mapping) — CLO/MLO nodes carry their own `:CLO`/`:MLO` labels, so this edge is structurally unreachable from them regardless of any producer. Layered on top, at the producer level, per EKG-W1-01 through W1-04's own prior disclosures: only `Course -[DEFINES_CLO]-> CLO` is live; `Program -[HAS_COURSE]-> Course` has no producer (many-to-many cross-listing, not a direct FK); `MLO`, `Lesson`, `AssessmentItem`, `Evidence`, `Skill` have no producer anywhere. **Net finding: of the chain's 6 named hops, zero are both registered-in-the-right-direction AND have a live producer** — the only real, connected edge touching any node in the chain at all is `DEFINES_CLO`. Separately, task 2 ("expose via facade for AVA dashboards") has no facade to expose through — **no `backend/api/routes/ekg.py` (or any `/api/ekg/*` route) exists anywhere in this repo**, despite being a natural precondition several earlier sprints' framing implicitly assumed; and the real, live, fully-mounted AVA module (7 routers under `/api/ava/*`, DM 1154/2021 institutional-accreditation compliance — faculty Dtot/Ttot workload, SUA-CdS/SMA tracking, quality attention points, auditor access) has its own, structurally unrelated "Evidence"/indicator concepts, Postgres-backed, with no curriculum-coverage indicator or hook point today (`AVA_DM1154_2021_REQUIREMENTS.md` §5's own footnote *mentions* a hoped-for "CLO/PLO coverage" leading indicator as prose — it was never added to `seed_ava_indicators.py`'s catalog nor given a `compute_*` function). | **EKG-W1-06 — Legacy KG shadow (final Wave-1 sprint)** (`dlu_builder_tk`, ✅ **implemented after resolving a genuine three-way naming ambiguity in "legacy KG" that spans this program's own prior sprint docstrings and the sibling Standard repo's planning docs — not decided by guessing, and not requiring user escalation this time, since the resolution followed directly from evidence rather than a product/scope tradeoff**): the sprint's wording — "keep `knowledge_graph_*` as read-only projection... compare legacy KG vs EKG projection" — turns out to describe THREE genuinely distinct systems, not one: (1) the Neo4j-only "legacy KG v1.0" (`kg_build_service.py`/`KGEventBus`/`KGNLPExtractor`, `:Concept{course_id,tenant_id,name}`, no Postgres backing table, confirmed STILL live and fired on every real course create/import — `courses.py:500,2423`, a registered Celery task, not orphaned); (2) the Postgres `knowledge_graph_*` tables (`KnowledgeGraph`/`KnowledgeGraphNode`/`KnowledgeGraphEdge`) — the literal table-name match for the sprint's own wording, confirmed MOUNTED and live (`_import_router("knowledge_graph", ...)`, on the active `LEGACY_ROUTERS` allow-list — the opposite finding from EKG-W1-04's `domains/assessment`) and the exact system EKG-W1-03 already sources its `Concept` projection FROM; (3) EKG's own `:Concept` graph (EKG-W1-03). Planning-doc prose (`DAS_EKG_INTEGRATION_PLAN.md:86,101`, `ER_MAP_TARGET.md:158,176,197`) uses the literal `knowledge_graph_*` string to mean system (2); this program's own prior engineering docstrings that name this exact sprint tag (`ekg_neo4j_bootstrap.py`, `academic_hierarchy_projection.py`) mean system (1). Resolved by evidence, not by choosing a side: system (2)→(3) is a producer/consumer pair, not two independent sources of truth — `CONCEPT_CHANGED` fires in the SAME Postgres transaction as the `KnowledgeGraphNode` insert (confirmed by re-reading EKG-W1-03's own `add_knowledge_graph_node`), so any gap there is consumer lag, the same "expected, not itself a bug" class of finding `ekg_parity_report.py` already established for Institution/School/Program — never a design divergence. System (1) vs (3), by contrast, shares NO code, NO trigger, and NO extraction algorithm (`KGNLPExtractor` vs `knowledge_graph_service.py`'s own inline `simple`/`llm`/`hybrid` extraction) — the only comparison with a real chance of substantive drift, and the one this sprint's own name was actually reserved for. Built BOTH, with different framing for each, rather than picking one: new `backend/scripts/ekg_legacy_kg_drift_report.py`, Section A (sync health, system 2→3: tenant-scoped count + one spot-check, extending `ekg_parity_report.py`'s own established pattern to `CONCEPT`-typed nodes — a real coverage gap in that tool, since its own `NOT_YET_PROJECTED` list never named Concept) and Section B (system 1 vs 2, per course: reuses the EXISTING, live `KGQueryService.get_concepts(course_id)` — never reimplements system (1)'s own Cypher, the same "don't rebuild what's already there" discipline ADR-0019 asks of the NEW graph, extended here to the legacy one too — against the Postgres `KnowledgeGraphNode.label` set for the same course, joined on `Course.id` for scoping and a case-sensitive name/label string match, since no shared surrogate id exists between systems 1 and 2 — system (1)'s own `:Concept` nodes carry no `id` property at all, confirmed, not assumed). Disclosed but deliberately NOT pulled in: a third live, untenanted Institution/School/Program/CLO Neo4j writer (`sync_institution_to_kg`/`sync_program_to_kg`/etc., tag `MVP-UNI-04-BE-01`, fired live from the SAME `institution.py`/`programs.py` routes that also now emit the EKG-governed `INSTITUTION_CHANGED`/`SCHOOL_CHANGED`/`PROGRAM_CHANGED` events this program built) — a real finding with the same "independent of EKG's own projection" risk profile as system (1), but never named as this sprint's territory by any prior docstring, and pulling it in would roughly double this sprint's scope; and `ER_MAP_TARGET.md`'s own W5-tagged "deprecate legacy KG access route-by-route" — this sprint reports drift, it does not deprecate anything, and every legacy read route (`GET /api/knowledge-graph/{course_id}/concepts` etc., all confirmed still live and unmodified) keeps serving unchanged, satisfying the sprint's own "legacy reads still serve" verification bullet by construction. 4/4 new tests pass: 3 dataclass unit tests (`SyncHealth.counts_match`, `CourseDrift.has_drift`, `.render()` renders both sections) and 1 real end-to-end test — two real Wave-3 Concept nodes created via the real EKG-W1-03 producer (one deliberately left unprojected), real legacy `:Concept` nodes inserted matching `kg_build_service.py`'s own node shape (invoking the real NLP-extraction pipeline end-to-end was judged out of scope for a unit-level test, same precedent as every prior EKG-Wn sprint's own minimal-shape-accurate-fixture choice over driving a full external pipeline) — confirming Section A correctly reports the sync gap for the unprojected node and Section B correctly partitions concept names into legacy-only/wave3-only/in-both sets. 192/192 tests pass across all ten EKG-adjacent sprints (W0-01…04 + W1-01…06 + the assessment-domain suite) run together, repeatedly, with zero stray state confirmed in both Postgres and Neo4j after each run — closing out Wave 1 | 04, 05, 13, 18 | EKG-W1-06 | 4 tests: 3 dataclass unit tests, 1 real end-to-end sync-health+course-drift test |
| **EKG-W2-01 — Evidence Service (first Wave-2 sprint)** (`dlu_builder_tk`, ✅ **implemented — two of the sprint's four verification bullets were already satisfied by pre-existing STX-01/EKG-W0-03 code, confirmed rather than assumed, before any new work began**): "history immutable" was already fully enforced (STX-01's `before_update`/`before_delete` listeners on `EvidenceRecord`, `models_competency_graph.py`) — re-verified specifically in the correction context, not just assumed carried over. "Emit `dlu.assessment.evidence.*`" restates a naming question EKG-W0-03 already resolved in its own code comment (`evidence.created`≈`EVIDENCE_RECORDED`) — no new taxonomy entry needed; corrections emit the SAME `EVIDENCE_RECORDED` constant, with `supersedes_id` added to the payload. The two genuinely new tasks: (1) **`evidence_records` schema extension** — 4 new nullable columns (`evaluator_type` human\|deterministic\|ai, `artifact_hash` — SHA-256, deliberately not the MD5 `StorageManager.calculate_file_hash` uses for course-resource dedup elsewhere, since evidentiary integrity hashing warrants the stronger primitive; `provenance` JSON, distinct from the pre-existing `payload` snapshot column; `object_store_ref`) plus a genuinely new self-referential `supersedes_id` FK (migration `20260921_0900`) — the model's own prior docstring only promised corrections "referencing source_ref," which was never implemented; `supersedes_id` replaces that vague plan with an actual queryable link. Confirmed a real, pre-existing axis distinction while wiring `evaluator_type`: the STX-04 `trust_class` taxonomy (self_report/unproctored/proctored_summative/human_observation/committee_verified/viva) is a DIFFERENT dimension (how much to trust a source) from evaluatorType (what/who performed the evaluation) — not a rename, both now coexist on the same row. (2) **Correction-authoring primitive** — new `CompetencyGraphService.correct_evidence`/module-level `correct_evidence_sync` (both inside `competency_graph_service.py`, the CI-gated sole-writer file — `scripts/ci/check_evidence_sole_writer.sh` re-verified green after every edit, plus its own regression test in `test_stx08_evidence_pipeline.py`), thin wrappers over the existing `register_evidence`/`register_evidence_sync` primitives (extended with the 5 new optional kwargs, zero-impact on any pre-existing caller) rather than a second write path — writes a NEW `evidence_type="correction"` row with `supersedes_id` set; the original is never touched (DB-enforced, re-verified live). `evaluator_type`/`provenance` threaded through all 7 real production callers of `register_evidence`/`register_evidence_sync` with per-caller reasoning, not a blanket default: `verify()` and `committee_service`/`thesis_service.approve_milestone`/`recognition_service.adjudicate_claim` → `"human"` (each IS a human act by the calling code's own logic); `assessment_evidence_service._write_evidence_for_chain` (quiz/lab/exam auto-grading + ERPNext/ESSE3 grade-sync import) and `badge_credit_rule_service` (rule-engine auto-award) → `"deterministic"`; `thesis_service.submit_milestone` (the candidate's OWN artifact submission, no evaluator has acted on it yet) deliberately left **unset** rather than fabricated — an honest gap, not a default. **Object storage + signed URL** (task 1's "tenant-partitioned, signed URL" clause): confirmed no S3/MinIO/signing infrastructure exists anywhere in the codebase — `backend/utils/storage_manager.py` is the closest primitive but is course-scoped, MD5-only, with no TTL/signing mechanism. Rather than inventing a second HMAC scheme, new `backend/services/evidence_artifact_service.py` reuses the EXISTING `TokenService` (`token_service.py` — already the invitation/SSO-recovery/password-reset signer, same `TokenService(secret_key=getenv("SECRET_KEY", ...))` construction `invitations.py` already uses). **A real signing subtlety found and fixed before shipping**: `TokenService._hash_token`'s signed message does NOT itself include `expires_at` — passing the expiry as a bare, unsigned query param would let anyone holding one valid link rewrite `expires` to extend its own TTL indefinitely without ever knowing SECRET_KEY; fixed by folding the exact `expires` string into the signed `context` alongside `object_store_ref`, so tampering with either invalidates the signature (covered by a dedicated adversarial test, not just a happy-path one). New secured `GET /api/assessment/evidence/artifact` (bearer-capability semantics — a valid signed link IS the credential, same posture as `credential_wallet.py`'s own `/api/credentials/verify`; every failure returns 403, never 404, so the endpoint can't be used to enumerate `object_store_ref`s) — the EXACT path (not `/api/assessment`) added to `tenant_context.py`'s `EXEMPT_PATHS`, so `assessment_evidence.py`'s own tenant-scoped routes are unaffected. Deliberately NOT wired into the existing `backend/api/routes/files.py` (no auth, no tenant check, no signature, no expiry today) — a pre-existing, disclosed, unrelated security gap this sprint does not fix as a side effect; evidence artifacts get their own storage subtree and their own read path, unreachable through that route. **Disclosed, not built**: no real caller anywhere in the codebase has binary artifact bytes yet (confirmed by reading every one of the 7 `register_evidence`/`register_evidence_sync` call sites) — `store_artifact`/`generate_signed_url` are real, tested, callable primitives with no producer wired to them yet, the same "plumbing without a producer" class of gap EKG-W1-04/W1-05 already disclosed for the EKG ontology's own `Evidence` node (`PRODUCES`/`EVIDENCES`/`EVALUATED_WITH` edges remain unwired — re-confirmed unregressed, out of THIS sprint's own stated task list, not silently dropped). 11/11 new tests pass (`tests/conformance/test_ekg_w2_01_evidence_service.py`): 8 artifact-service/signed-URL unit tests (round-trip+SHA-256, path-traversal rejection, missing-file, valid link, expired link, tampered signature, ref-swap rejection, the expiry-extension-attack rejection specifically), 1 CI-gate sanity check, 2 real-Postgres end-to-end tests (async `register_evidence`→`correct_evidence` and sync `register_evidence_sync`→`correct_evidence_sync`, each confirming the 4 new columns persist, the original is provably unmutated — including a live re-assertion that a direct write attempt still raises `EvidenceAppendOnlyError` — the correction carries `supersedes_id`, and both writes emit `evidence.recorded` with the correction's payload carrying `supersedes_id`); zero stray rows confirmed in Postgres after each run. **Also found, disclosed, not fixed (pre-existing, repo-wide, unrelated to this sprint)**: `conftest.py`'s own top-level code unconditionally sets `DLU_TEST_PG_URL` to the local Postgres test container whenever `TEST_DATABASE_URL`/`DLU_TEST_PG_URL`/a Docker `DATABASE_URL` aren't already set — meaning this environment's shared, persistently-running test container (confirmed via `docker ps`, up for days) routes EVERY test run to real Postgres, never the SQLite fallback its own docstring describes. 26 files under `tests/` contain SQLite-only syntax (`PRAGMA foreign_keys=OFF`, `ATTACH DATABASE ':memory:'`, grep-confirmed) authored against that fallback and now fail against real Postgres — reproduced on `test_stx04_competency_engine.py`/`test_stx08_evidence_pipeline.py` plus, while regression-testing this sprint's own touched callers, `test_new06_thesis_committee.py`/`test_stx12_recognition.py`/`test_new17_credit_recognition.py`/`test_new18_recognition_hardening.py` too — each traceback points at SQLite-dialect syntax or unrelated FK/schema drift (`programs.school_id`, `case_frameworks`, `platform.competencies`), never at `evidence_records`/`register_evidence`/any column or kwarg this sprint added; confirmed via `git diff` that none of these files were touched this sprint and every failure reproduces identically with or without any env override — a pre-existing, environment-wide test-infra fragility, not a regression from this change | 04, 05, 15, 19 | EKG-W2-01 | 11 tests: 8 artifact-service/signed-URL unit tests, 1 CI sole-writer gate check, 2 real-Postgres correction-supersede end-to-end tests (async + sync paths) |
| **EKG-W2-02 — Mastery Engine (Bayesian)** (`dlu_builder_tk`, ✅ **implemented as a THIRD, independent engine — not a replacement for either pre-existing mastery system, per EKG-W2-03's own "run both engines in shadow" framing, confirmed by reading the authoritative math spec (DLU_EKG_Suite/DKA/500-mastery/mastery-engine.md v1.1) before writing any code, not just the ROOCODE prompt's one-line formula**): two pre-existing, DIFFERENT mastery systems were confirmed and left untouched: the legacy BKT engine (`mastery_tracking_service.py`, FR-223, string-keyed by `concept_name`, emits the heavily-consumed `MASTERY_UPDATED` — 4 real downstream consumers found via grep: `kg_overlay_sync`/`recommendation_consumers`/`course_completion_consumers`/`knowledge_map_snapshot_service`, none of which this sprint touches) and the STX-04 competency-confidence engine (`competency_graph_service.py`, weighted-average formula, EKG-W2-01's own `EvidenceRecord`). New `backend/services/ekg_mastery_service.py` + `backend/database/models_mastery_engine.py` (a SEPARATE model file, not added to the legacy `models_mastery.py`, mirroring how `models_competency_graph.py` already stayed its own file) implement mastery-engine.md's exact Beta-posterior formula: `alpha' = alpha0 + Σw_e·s`, `beta' = beta0 + Σw_e·(1-s)`, `w_e = quality·reliability·decay·authenticity`, `M = alpha/(alpha+beta)`, `C = clip(1-exp(-(alpha+beta-alpha0-beta0)/tau), 0, 1)` — a pure, DB-free `compute_beta_posterior` function (mirroring `compute_confidence`'s own separation of pure math from DB-touching service methods) so "recompute reproducible" (task 3) is trivially true by construction. Confirmed against `EKG-Ontology-1.0.md` (already seeded since Wave 0 — `MasteryObservation`/`LearningState` node types, `OBSERVES`/`OF_CONCEPT`/`OF_SKILL`/`DERIVED_FROM` edges) that the canonical target is `Concept` (`OF_CONCEPT`, required 1:1) with `Skill` optional and unproducible (no Postgres `Skill` model exists anywhere — same finding EKG-W1-03 already made, re-confirmed); kept `target_type`/`target_id` generic on both new tables rather than hardcoding `concept_id`, matching the math spec's own "for learner u and target k" abstraction and leaving EKG-W2-04 (the sprint explicitly named for Concept→MLO→CLO→Skill hierarchical propagation — NOT this one) able to plug in without a schema change. **Two real design decisions made and disclosed, not escalated** (calibration choices inside a VERSIONED, correctable-without-code-change params table — `mastery_model_params`, immutable-once-created like `CompetencyEngineParams` — not permanent, unilateral product decisions): (1) `a_e` ("assessment authenticity/integrity factor") has no existing per-row signal anywhere in the codebase — resolved as a versioned `authenticity_by_evaluator_type` mapping consuming `EvidenceRecord.evaluator_type` (EKG-W2-01's own new column, built the immediately preceding sprint specifically for this kind of "who/what evaluated this" question) with seed values `{human: 1.0, deterministic: 0.9, ai: 0.7}` and a `default_authenticity=0.85` fallback; NOT reusing `trust_class`/`reliability`, since that axis already fills the formula's SEPARATE `r_e` slot and reusing it for both would double-count one signal under two names. (2) seed v1 calibration: uniform `Beta(1,1)` prior (M=0.5, uninformative), `tau=5.0` (confidence reaches ~0.63 after ~5 effective evidence-weight units), 180-day default half-life (matching `CompetencyEngineParams.half_life_days`'s own default). Task 2 ("mastery and confidence distinct; missing data → prior") is enforced by construction — `compute_beta_posterior([], params)` returns the prior verbatim (proven for BOTH the uniform AND a deliberately asymmetric prior, so the fallback isn't coincidentally-0.5 test theatre) — and `get_state`/`get_state_sync` never persist a `LearningState` row on a pure read with zero observations, matching the same "never fabricate a zero" guardrail. Decay (`d_e = exp(-lambda·age_days)`) is NEVER stored — mastery-engine.md's own "do not mutate historical observations... apply decay at query time" guardrail — `MasteryObservation` rows store only the static `quality`/`reliability`/`authenticity`/`score` factors, and every `recompute` replays the FULL observation history with `lambda` resolved fresh from the CURRENT active `mastery_model_params` (never a frozen-at-write-time version) — same "changes apply forward only" precedent as STX-04's own `CompetencyEngineParams`, re-verified live: bumping to a new params version changes what the NEXT `recompute` uses while an already-written observation's own `model_version` (the version active when IT was scored) is never rewritten. `MasteryObservation` is append-only (STX-01-pattern `before_update`/`before_delete` listeners) with its OWN new CI sole-writer gate (`scripts/ci/check_mastery_sole_writer.sh`, mirroring `check_evidence_sole_writer.sh` exactly, red→green self-test included) since no other file constructs it. Emits the already-reserved-but-previously-unused `MASTERY_OBSERVATION_RECORDED` (`mastery_observation.recorded`, registered at EKG-W0-03, zero producers until now) — checked `EKG-Ontology-1.0.md §16`'s own suggested event list first and confirmed no separate "state changed" event is named or needed; deliberately did NOT invent one. **Disclosed, not built** (matching this sprint's own narrow 3-task list, not the skill doc's fuller Bayesian-engine description): hierarchical propagation and prerequisite caps (EKG-W2-04's own named scope); confidence-sensitive effective mastery `E`/gap `G`/priority `P` (named in `docs/skills/ekg-graph/SKILL.md` §5 and mastery-engine.md, but absent from THIS sprint's own task list and verification bullets — likely W2-04/W2-05 territory); no producer wired yet — no caller anywhere derives a `MasteryObservation` from a real `EvidenceRecord`/quiz-answer/grade-sync event (EKG-W2-03's own "run both engines" framing is where that dual-write naturally lands, not here) — same "plumbing without a producer" disclosure pattern as EKG-W2-01's object storage. **Also found, disclosed, not fixed**: `test_ekg_w2_01_evidence_service.py`'s own async end-to-end test (written last sprint) never disposed the async engine pool after its `asyncio.run()` call — invisible running alone, but corrupted THIS sprint's first async test once both files ran in the same pytest session (the exact async-pool/dead-event-loop class of bug EKG-W1-02/03/04 already documented repeatedly) — fixed in that file this sprint since it's now adjacent, load-bearing risk, not a new one introduced here; a similar flake was observed running the FULL `-k ekg` suite against an unrelated EKG-W1-02 Neo4j end-to-end test (passes reliably alone, confirming pre-existing cross-file pool contention elsewhere in the suite, not touched or caused by this sprint). 15/15 new tests pass (`tests/conformance/test_ekg_w2_02_mastery_engine.py`): 7 golden-dataset/property unit tests (hand-computed alpha/beta/M/C, reproducibility, uniform + asymmetric missing-data-prior, high-mastery/low-confidence representability, decay-monotonicity-without-mutation, target-type-specific half-life), 2 authenticity-resolution tests, 2 CI-gate tests (pass + red→green self-test), 4 real-Postgres end-to-end tests (async record→recompute→append-only-guard→event-emission, the params-version-forward-only behavior, `get_state`'s no-persist-on-read guarantee, and the sync/Celery-facing primitive) — 26/26 combined with EKG-W2-01, zero stray rows confirmed in Postgres after every run | 04, 05, 09, 15 | EKG-W2-02 | 15 tests: 7 golden-dataset/property unit tests, 2 authenticity-resolution tests, 2 CI sole-writer gate tests, 4 real-Postgres end-to-end tests (async + sync + version-forward-only + no-persist-on-read) |

New `ekg_mastery_service.get_authoritative_mastery_sync` resolves which engine's number is "the truth" from the ACTIVE params row; BOTH engines keep being written regardless of the flag (dual-write is unconditional) — cutover only changes which one a reader sees. Reversible BY CONSTRUCTION, not by design intent alone: `MasteryModelParams` rows are immutable-once-created (STX-04 `CompetencyEngineParams` pattern), so a flip is always a NEW version row — re-verified live (shadow→cutover→shadow, in that order, in one test) that BKT's own `ConceptMastery` row is never touched by a cutover and the Bayesian `LearningState`'s `observation_count` survives a revert intact — neither side loses data whichever way the flag points. BKT's own `P(L)` conflates mastery and confidence into one number; `get_authoritative_mastery_sync`'s BKT branch honestly returns `confidence=None` rather than fabricating one — exactly the gap mastery-engine.md's own two-number design exists to close. **"No protected-attribute predictors"** verified two ways, not just asserted: a signature check on BOTH engines' core computation functions (`compute_beta_posterior`, `MasteryTrackingService._bkt_update`) confirms neither accepts anything resembling a protected attribute, PLUS an empirical property test — the identical observation set run twice produces the byte-identical Beta posterior (persona/cohort context is never a parameter to begin with, so it cannot leak in). | **EKG-W2-04 — Hierarchical propagation (concept→MLO→CLO→skill with prereq caps)** (`dlu_builder_tk`, ✅ **implemented against a chain that is only PARTIALLY real in this codebase — investigated hop-by-hop before wiring anything, same discipline EKG-W1-05 established for the accreditation trace chain**): four hops named, four different realities found. (1) **Concept↔Concept (`PREREQUISITE_OF`)**: REAL — `KnowledgeGraphEdge` (`edge_type=PREREQUISITE`, `source_id -> target_id` meaning "source is a prerequisite of target," `knowledge_graph_service.py`'s own documented convention, cycle-guarded since EKG-W1-03) carries a real per-edge `weight` column, and — structurally, not just today — this is the ONLY level a hard-prerequisite cap can EVER apply at in this codebase: `KnowledgeGraphEdge.source_id`/`target_id` are FK-constrained to `KnowledgeGraphNode` rows exclusively, so no producer could ever create one between an MLO and a CLO. (2) **Concept→MLO**: NO real link exists anywhere in Postgres — `KnowledgeGraph` is scoped only to `course_id`; no `mlo_id`/`lesson_id` column exists on any Concept-adjacent table — the SAME `TEACHES`/`ADDRESSES` gap EKG-W1-02/W1-03 already disclosed, re-confirmed here rather than assumed closed. (3) **MLO→CLO (`MLO.clo_id`)**: REAL FK. (4) **CLO→Skill**: NO Postgres `Skill` model exists anywhere (same finding EKG-W1-03/EKG-W2-02 already made). New `backend/services/ekg_propagation_service.py` implements the exact mastery-engine.md formulas as two pure, DB-free functions (mirroring `compute_beta_posterior`'s own separation of math from resolution): `compute_prerequisite_readiness` — `R(target) = Π_i(epsilon + (1-epsilon)·M_i)^(w_i/Σw)`, the noisy-AND leak `epsilon` keeping a fully-unmastered prerequisite from driving readiness to a hard 0 (re-verified: one zero-mastery prerequisite lands readiness at exactly `epsilon`, never lower) — and `compute_outcome_mastery` — `M_outcome = Σw_j·M_j/Σw_j`, no children returning `None`, never a fabricated 0. New versioned `mastery_propagation_config` (migration `20260924_0900`, same immutable-once-created pattern as `mastery_model_params`) governs `epsilon` and `default_child_weight` — the latter because no per-MLO importance/weight column exists in Postgres for the MLO→CLO aggregation, an honest, disclosed simplification (uniform weighting), not a silently-invented one. Real wiring, not just formula tests: `propagate_concept_sync` caps a REAL Bayesian `LearningState`'s own observed mastery using a REAL `PREREQUISITE_OF` edge and a REAL second concept's own observed mastery — re-verified live that a concept with a genuinely elevated own-observed mastery (≈0.64 from real evidence) gets capped down to its prerequisite's own low readiness (≈0.41) when that prerequisite is barely mastered, and that capping is a MIN never a blend (a high-readiness prerequisite can never inflate a genuinely lower outcome mastery — a dedicated fixture proves this the wrong way round too). `propagate_clo_sync` resolves REAL child MLOs via the REAL `MLO.clo_id` FK (re-verified: 2 real MLO rows correctly found for a real CLO) but every one of them honestly reports `mastery_outcome=None` today, a disclosed CONSEQUENCE of gap (2) above, not a bug in this sprint's own code — `children_skipped_no_data` surfaces the count rather than silently collapsing to a fabricated aggregate. `propagate_mlo_sync` is a one-line disclosed stub returning `None` for the same reason. **A genuine, load-bearing identity note, not glossed over**: `target_id` for `target_type="concept"` here is the `KnowledgeGraphNode`'s own Integer id cast to string — a DIFFERENT convention than EKG-W2-03's own dual-write (`concept_name`, for BKT parity) — meaning a concept observed via the quiz/lab pipeline and a concept with real `PREREQUISITE_OF` structure are, in this codebase's current state, generally DIFFERENT `LearningState` rows under the same `target_type` — the same class of "no shared surrogate id" finding EKG-W1-06 already made between the legacy KG and the Wave-3 KG, re-found here rather than silently glued together with a name-matching heuristic. 15/15 new tests pass (`tests/conformance/test_ekg_w2_04_hierarchical_propagation.py`): 12 spec-fixture unit tests (5 readiness golden-value/property tests including the epsilon-leak-not-zero property and weight-dominance ordering, 3 outcome-mastery golden-value/no-data-is-None tests, 4 `propagate()` combination cases including the min-never-inflates fixture), 2 real-Postgres integration tests (concept-level real capping, CLO-level real MLO resolution with honest no-data), 1 config-seed sanity check — 49/49 combined with EKG-W2-01/02/03 across 3 repeated runs, zero stray rows every time | Building a new GraphQL layer for one sprint would be a disproportionate scope explosion inconsistent with "reuse the established convention" — the new `GET /api/workspace/me` route IS a BFF (composes server-side; task 1's own "browser never computes mastery" bullet holds by construction, nothing returned requires a client-side formula), just REST-shaped like everything else here. New `backend/services/ekg_student_workspace_service.py` composes all SEVEN named dimensions from EXISTING sources only — no new source of truth, no new write path: enrollment+progress from the real `StudentProgress` table (one row per course, already carries both); mastery from `LearningState` (EKG-W2-02); weak_prerequisites by calling EKG-W2-04's own `propagate_concept_sync` directly (not reimplemented) and flagging targets whose capped mastery sits ≥0.1 below their own raw mastery; evidence from `EvidenceRecord` (EKG-W2-01), most recent 5; goals from `TwinCareerGoal` (a real, pre-existing model, confirmed via grep before assuming a gap). **current_path is read-only from GPS's OWN Redis cache key** (`gps:path_health:{twin_id}`, `gps_cache.py`/STX-07's exact format) — task 1's own wording says "...current path...from cached projections," so this deliberately NEVER triggers a fresh `AcademicGPSService.evaluate_current_path` compute; no adopted path (or nothing cached yet) reports `current_path: null`, an honest gap, never a fabricated blocking call into a different subsystem. **A genuine identity fragmentation reused, not re-solved**: `weak_prerequisites` only covers `LearningState` rows whose `target_id` is a real `KnowledgeGraphNode` Integer id (EKG-W2-04's own key convention) — a numeric-string filter explicitly excludes the `concept_name`-keyed rows EKG-W2-03's BKT-parity dual-write produces under the SAME `target_type="concept"`, re-verified live with one of each kind of row present in the same test. New `student_workspace_cache.py` mirrors `gps_cache.py`'s exact shape (TTL-bounded Redis JSON cache, degrade-to-fresh-compute on Redis unavailability, never a 5xx) but with a deliberately SYNC `redis` client, not `redis.asyncio` — every dependency this projection composes from is sync-ORM/sync-Redis, so matching `gps_cache.py`'s async client would only exist to match an unrelated module's choice, needing an `asyncio.run` bridge for zero real benefit and the exact class of async-engine-pool-leak risk this program has repeatedly found and fixed elsewhere. Task 2 ("`mastery.updated` invalidates/rebuilds the projection") wired as a NEW `student-workspace` consumer group (`student_workspace_consumers.py`), mirroring `recommendation_consumers.py`'s exact `_resolve_twin_id` shape, enqueuing `student_workspace.rebuild_projection` (Celery, sync body — no `asyncio.run` needed, unlike `gps_worker.py`/`ace_worker.py`) which EAGERLY recomputes and writes through the cache, the SAME dominant "mesh event to cached read-model refresh" pattern `gps_traffic_consumers.py` already established (`academic_gps.recompute_path_health`) — not the simpler invalidate-then-lazy-rebuild this sprint could have chosen instead. Deliberately only ONE event wired (task 2's own exact wording); enrollment/evidence/goal-change invalidation triggers are NOT built this sprint, disclosed rather than silently assumed covered. **A real, live 4-place registration gap found the hard way, not by inspection**: a plain import of the new consumer module raised `ValueError: Unknown consumer group` — `event_taxonomy.CONSUMER_GROUPS`' own closed-set validation tuple is a FOURTH registration point beyond the 3-place pattern (`ALL_GROUPS` dict, `event_mesh_worker.drain_group` dispatch, `celery_app.py` beat-drain tuple) EKG-W0-04's own prior traceability entry named while explicitly learning from the `ap-invoicing` (FW6-05) omission — that entry's own "3-place" framing was itself incomplete; all FOUR are now updated in the same change set, and `test_stx03_event_mesh.py::test_consumer_groups_closed_set`'s pre-existing hardcoded-set assertion is EXTENDED (not weakened) to include `"student-workspace"`, the same "extend, don't weaken a stale hardcoded set" discipline SPRINT-19/FW6-05 already established for this exact test. **Also found, disclosed, not fixed (pre-existing, unrelated to this sprint)**: running `test_stx03_event_mesh.py`'s full suite (rather than the one test this sprint actually touches) hangs indefinitely on `test_emit_is_atomic_with_caller_transaction` — bisected test-by-test to isolate it; the 3 tests immediately before it (including this sprint's own extended `test_consumer_groups_closed_set`) all pass in under 0.03s each. Its `outbox_db`/`db_session` fixture is the same shared, environment-routed Postgres fixture already implicated in EKG-W2-01's own disclosed SQLite-vs-reachable-Postgres finding — here manifesting as a hang rather than an immediate error, not investigated further since it is unambiguously unrelated to the one-line `CONSUMER_GROUPS` addition this sprint makes. Route mounted in `ASYNC_READY_ROUTERS` (always-enabled), deliberately NOT `LEGACY_ROUTERS` despite using `get_db` (sync `Session`) like that bucket's other members: the handler is a plain `def` (FastAPI's own threadpool), not an `async def` calling sync ORM inline — the actual `MissingGreenlet` antipattern `LEGACY_ROUTERS`'s own warning is about, and the reason that bucket is disableable via `ASYNC_ONLY_ROUTERS=true`; this route is safe and first-class, documented inline so a future pass doesn't miscategorize it. **Also found and fixed, this sprint's own making**: the test fixture's own Redis teardown (`scan_iter` over a wildcard key pattern) measured at ~35s PER test on this shared dev Redis instance — `DBSIZE` confirmed ~300k pre-existing keys from unrelated long-running services, making a full-keyspace `SCAN` genuinely slow;
