| **EKG-W2-06 — Learning Path MVP (final Wave-2 sprint)** (`dlu_builder_tk`, ✅ **implemented as a THIRD "recommend what's next" system — checked against BOTH pre-existing ones before writing any code, same discipline EKG-W2-02 used for BKT vs Bayesian mastery**): `recommendation_engine.py` (CCP-P2-BE-03) is admin-authored RULE-based (trigger conditions to actions, no scored ranking at all); `recommendation_service_v2.py` (STX-09, BOOK-13 Ch. 5-6) is a mature three-stage pipeline with its OWN deterministic formula (`score = w1*gap_relevance + w2*path_alignment + w3*engagement_fit + w4*urgency`) over course/content candidates. Neither computes this sprint's own literal formula, and neither reads EKG-W2-02's Bayesian mastery/confidence or EKG-W2-04's propagation-capped readiness at all — new `backend/services/ekg_learning_path_service.py` is genuinely new and EKG-native, reusing those two Wave-2 outputs directly rather than re-deriving them. Implements task 1's formula verbatim, not reparameterized: `utility = gapCoverage*expectedGain*confidenceNeed*relevance − timePenalty − redundancy`. Each of the four multiplicative factors is this service's OWN disclosed mapping onto real signals, not a fabricated metric: `expectedGain` = one minus EKG-W2-04's own propagation-CAPPED mastery (not the raw value — already accounts for prerequisite gating); `confidenceNeed` = one minus EKG-W2-02's own Beta-posterior confidence, a DELIBERATELY separate axis from expectedGain (Mastery ≠ Confidence, ekg-graph skill §1 — a candidate can be far from mastered yet already well-understood-to-be-far, or the reverse); `relevance` = governed `relevance_enrolled`/`relevance_not_enrolled` keyed on EKG-W2-05's own enrollment read (`StudentProgress`); `gapCoverage` = prerequisite CENTRALITY (ekg-graph skill §5's own term) — how many OTHER weak candidates in the same call have this one as a real `PREREQUISITE_OF` dependency (`ekg_propagation_service.resolve_concept_dependents_sync`, new, the REVERSE query of EKG-W2-04's own prerequisite lookup), reused not reimplemented. **A real planning-doc-vs-operational-spec mismatch checked and resolved, not silently ignored**: `ER_MAP_TARGET.md` itself describes `learning_paths`/`learning_path_steps` as new relational tables that are "GPS output artefacts... extends `route_graph_versions`/`path_scenarios`" — implying a persisted table tightly coupled to Academic GPS's own course-sequencing system. The ACTUAL operational spec (`docs/ROOCODE_EKG_PROMPTS.md`, the doc every prior EKG-Wn sprint has treated as authoritative over the more aspirational ER_MAP_TARGET.md when the two diverge) names a NEW, EKG-prefixed file (`ekg_learning_path_service.py`) with its own formula that has nothing to do with GPS's own concerns (`GPSTarget`, `requirement_status`, `time_to_graduation`) — confirming this sprint's concept-level, GPS-independent design is the correct resolution, not an oversight of the ER_MAP_TARGET.md language. **A real formula bug found via the real-Postgres end-to-end test, not merely a fixture mismatch, and fixed before shipping**: the first version normalized `gapCoverage` to the plain [0,1] range (`dependent_count/max_dependent_count`) — zero for any LEAF concept (nothing depends on it). Since all four factors are MULTIPLIED, one zero factor zeroes the entire term regardless of how large a gap the leaf itself has — and leaf concepts are the MAJORITY in any real curriculum (only foundational concepts have many dependents), so that version would have excluded most genuine candidates as "low_utility." Fixed to `1.0 + (dependent_count/max_dependent_count)` (range [1.0, 2.0]) — covering one's own gap is real coverage and must never be zero; centrality only ever ADDS to that baseline. `timePenalty` (governed flat constant — no real per-concept study-time estimate exists anywhere in this codebase, disclosed as a deliberate v1 simplification) and `redundancy` (governed penalty, applied ONLY to caller-supplied `recent_target_ids` — deliberately DISTINCT from `exclude_target_ids`, a hard exclude for e.g. an explicitly-dismissed concept: redundancy deprioritizes, exclusion removes) are subtracted per the formula, not multiplied. New versioned `learning_path_config` (migration `20260925_0900`, same immutable-once-created pattern as `mastery_model_params`/`mastery_propagation_config`) governs only the derived-signal thresholds this service needed to invent (`mastery_threshold_for_exclusion`, the two relevance constants, `default_time_penalty`, `redundancy_penalty`, `min_utility_threshold`, `unready_prerequisite_readiness_threshold`) — the formula itself is NOT parameterized, since it's the sprint's own literal spec, not a calibration knob. **"Respects prerequisites" (verification bullet) is a real ordering constraint, not just a filter**: new `order_respecting_prerequisites`, a stable topological adjustment layered over the utility-sorted list — any candidate's own unready prerequisite (readiness below the governed threshold) that is ALSO a candidate is promoted to appear strictly before it; re-verified with a real 3-link prerequisite chain reordered from utility's worst-first order to the structurally-correct order, PLUS the inverse case (a ready prerequisite is never promoted, respecting utility ranking when there's nothing to fix) — termination is guaranteed by construction, not merely tested, since `KnowledgeGraphEdge` PREREQUISITE edges have been cycle-guarded at write time since EKG-W1-03, so the underlying dependency graph is structurally acyclic. A small, disclosed refactor accompanied this sprint: `_resolve_course_id_for_concept_node` (private, duplicated as EKG-W2-05's own local helper) is promoted to a shared, public `ekg_propagation_service.resolve_course_id_for_concept_node`, since EKG-W2-06 needed the identical lookup — re-verified the EKG-W2-04/W2-05 suites are unaffected by the refactor before building on top of it. **Disclosed, not built** (task 2 says "Return ordered path + explanation," not "persist one"): no `LearningPath`/`LearningPathStep` Postgres row is written, and the EKG Ontology's own AI-layer node types of the same names (registered since Wave 0) remain unprojected — this service recommends WHICH CONCEPTS to focus on next, not yet WHICH specific lesson or resource teaches them, since no real Concept-to-Lesson/Resource link exists in Postgres to resolve a step to actual content anyway (the same `TEACHES` gap EKG-W1-02/W1-03/W2-04/W2-05 have all already disclosed, re-confirmed rather than assumed closed here too). 14/14 new tests pass (`tests/conformance/test_ekg_w2_06_learning_path.py`): 6 pure-formula golden-value tests (including the fixed gapCoverage baseline-never-zero property), 6 `order_respecting_prerequisites` unit tests (no-op/promote/non-promote/outside-candidate-set/3-link-chain/determinism), 2 real-Postgres end-to-end tests (exclusion+ordering+relevance+algorithmVersion together on one real prerequisite pair plus a real already-mastered distractor plus a real caller-excluded candidate; and the redundancy penalty measurably lowering utility for an otherwise-identical recently-recommended candidate) — 74/74 combined with EKG-W2-01 through W2-05 across 3 repeated runs, zero stray rows every time — closing out Wave 2 | 04, 05, 09, 14, 17 | EKG-W2-06 | 14 tests: 6 pure-formula golden-value tests, 6 order_respecting_prerequisites unit tests, 2 real-Postgres end-to-end tests |

New `backend/database/models_ekg_vectors.py`/`ekg_vector_chunks` (migration `20260926_0900`, tenant schema, pgvector `Vector(1536)`) + `backend/services/ekg_vector_ingestion_service.py`, covering the sprint's own literal "resources/outcomes/concepts": `CourseResource.extracted_text` (real chunking via the existing, reused `SemanticChunker` — no `RAGAgent` dependency), `CLO`/`MLO.description`, and `KnowledgeGraphNode` (`node_type=CONCEPT`) label/description — each a single chunk except CourseResource. **"Approved" resolved to the one gate that actually exists**: none of the four source tables carries its own approval flag (confirmed by inspection before writing any query) — every ingestion function requires the OWNING `Course.status` to be `APPROVED`/`PUBLISHED`, raising `EkgVectorIngestionError` rather than silently skipping otherwise. **Two brand-new concepts with zero prior precedent** (confirmed by a repo-wide grep before writing this): `acl_tags`/`course_ids` are minimal and honest, not fabricated — `acl_tags = ["course:{course_id}"]`, `course_ids = [course_id]` (singleton today; JSON-array columns per the sprint's own plural naming, ready for future cross-course sharing no source table currently models). **Two-hop course_id resolution, found necessary rather than assumed**: neither `MLO` (only `clo_id`) nor `KnowledgeGraphNode` (only `graph_id`) carries its own `course_id` — resolved via the parent `CLO`/`KnowledgeGraph` row respectively, re-verified against real fixture data in the end-to-end tests. **Append-only** (same discipline as `EvidenceRecord`/`MasteryObservation`/`learning_path_config`, model-layer `EkgVectorChunkImmutableError` on update/delete): `version` is an ingestion-generation counter per `(tenant_id, source_type, source_id)` — none of the four source tables has its own "version" field, so re-ingesting a changed source appends generation N+1 rather than mutating history; re-verified a second ingestion of the same resource produces version-2 rows while version-1 rows remain untouched. **"Never embed confidential raw evidence" made structural, not a runtime filter**: `source_type` is a closed, CHECK-constrained 4-value vocabulary (`course_resource`/`clo`/`mlo`/`concept`) and the ingestion service contains no function reading `EvidenceRecord`/`StudentCompetency`/`MasteryObservation` at all — verified by an AST-based (not raw-substring, since the module's own docstring names those classes in prose) source-scan test. Reuses `rag_agent.py`'s own real embedding primitive (`_embed_texts`, `text-embedding-3-small`/1536 dims) rather than `academic_embedding_service.py`'s injected-`AsyncOpenAI` pattern — this module's closest sibling (`research_kb_service.py`) made the same choice, since `_embed_texts` is the one primitive in this codebase that already batches multiple chunks in one call. Real OpenAI calls in tests, no mocked embedding client (same "real, not fabricated" posture `test_research_workspace.py` already established for pgvector-backed suites). 11/11 new tests pass (`tests/conformance/test_ekg_w3_01_vector_ingestion.py`): closed-vocabulary + AST guardrail (2), course-resource metadata-completeness/unapproved-course-rejection/re-ingestion-versioning (3), CLO/MLO/concept ingestion + two-hop resolution + non-concept rejection (4), append-only update/delete rejection (2) — 649/657 in the full `tests/conformance/` regression run, zero residue; the 8 non-passing tests are pre-existing Wave-0/1/2 Neo4j-bootstrap/connectivity and Redis-default-port suites entirely unrelated to vector ingestion (untouched by this change, not investigated further as out of this sprint's scope) | 09, 11, 12 | EKG-W3-01 | 11 tests: 2 static (closed vocabulary + AST guardrail), 3 course-resource (metadata completeness, unapproved-course rejection, re-ingestion versioning), 4 outcomes/concepts (CLO, MLO two-hop, concept two-hop, non-concept rejection), 2 append-only (update/delete rejection) |
| **EKG-W3-02 — GraphRAG pipeline (second Wave-3 sprint)** (`dlu_builder_tk`, ✅ **implemented against a landscape investigated stage-by-stage before writing any code — six of the pipeline's eleven stages had a real-but-wrong or absent precedent, not a clean slate**): `kg_query_service.get_graphrag_context` already runs a real variable-length Cypher walk for GraphRAG-style context, but filters by `course_id` ONLY, never `tenantId` — a real tenant-isolation gap (`course_id` is a per-tenant-schema serial, not globally unique) left alone rather than reused; `kg_query_service.find_concept_by_name` is catalog-wide substring matching against the OLD legacy `:Concept` graph, by its own docstring's admission carrying no ACL; full-text search (`tsvector`/`ts_rank`) did not exist anywhere in this codebase; the ACP Gateway's chat-completion path (despite the branding) resolves to a DIRECT provider SDK call under shadow mode today, confirmed by reading `llm_client_factory.py` itself rather than trusting CLAUDE.md's own gate table (stale — `_compare_responses` is dead code, `get_client_for_agent`/`get_sync_client_for_agent` have no gateway branch at all); no redact-and-store companion to `moderation_service.detect_pii` existed for trace privacy; no generalized rerank utility existed beyond `recommendation_service_v2.py`'s own one-off weighted formula. New `bounded_neighborhood` (`named_query_templates.py`) — a fresh variable-length template against the NEW EKG ontology graph, `ALL(n IN nodes(path) WHERE n.tenantId = $tenantId)` over EVERY node the path touches (not just the two endpoints) — deliberately not reusing `get_graphrag_context`'s tenant-unsafe query. New `backend/database/models_graphrag.py` (migration `20260927_0900`): `graphrag_rerank_config` (governed, versioned, immutable, same shape as `recommendation_policy_params` — a real calibration surface) and `graphrag_traces` (append-only, same shape as `ace_cycle_traces`); `ekg_vector_chunks` gained a `chunk_text_tsv` Postgres-`GENERATED ALWAYS AS ... STORED` column (never an app write path, can't drift from `chunk_text`) + GIN index for the "optional full-text" stage. New `backend/services/ekg_graphrag_service.py`, `run_graphrag_pipeline`, implementing all eleven stages. **The four retrieval policies are plain code, not a governed table** (`POLICY_SOURCE_TYPES`) — a closed structural rule (which source types a policy allows), not a tunable number, same "formula itself is NOT parameterized" reasoning EKG-W2-06 already established; `CAREER_COACH`'s scope is honestly narrow (CLO/MLO outcome descriptions only — EKG-W3-01 embeds no Competency/ExternalCourse content yet), disclosed rather than fabricating a broader career-content system. **"Intent" is caller-declared policy selection**, not free-text NLP classification — no ML/LLM intent classifier exists anywhere in this codebase and the sprint's own deliverable is a fixed 4-value enum; an explicit `policy` argument is authoritative, a small disclosed keyword heuristic (`_INTENT_KEYWORDS`) picks a default only when none is given, and never silently escalates a non-faculty caller into `FACULTY_DESIGN` (falls back to `COURSE_GROUNDED` instead) — re-verified with an explicit-request rejection test too. **"Retrieval reduced to policy scope BEFORE search"** (verification bullet 1, literal): `PolicyScope.allowed_source_types` is pushed into the SQL `WHERE source_type IN (...)` clause of both `vector_search` (via pgvector-sqlalchemy's own `.cosine_distance()` comparator, not a hand-built vector literal) and `fulltext_search` — the expensive ranking work only ever runs over rows already scoped by tenant+source-type; `authorized_course_ids` narrows the result further via an in-memory intersection against each chunk's `course_ids` JSON array (disclosed: no portable SQL containment operator was judged worth it over an already tenant/type-narrowed, LIMIT-bounded window) — re-verified end-to-end with an identical-content chunk seeded into an out-of-scope course that never surfaces despite scoring identically on semantic distance, and a `CAREER_COACH` query that never returns a `course_resource` chunk despite one being tenant/course-authorized. **Grounding check is programmatic, not a second LLM call**: the model is instructed to cite bracketed passage numbers; `grounding_and_policy_checks` verifies every citation resolves to a real passage index (a hallucinated citation caps confidence at 0.3) and flags a non-empty context with zero citations as ungrounded — confidence is derived from top retrieval score + citation coverage, never a fabricated LLM self-rating (this codebase's own STX-11 already documents that pattern as unreliable). **LLM gateway call reuses `LLMClientFactory.get_client_for_agent`** — the real, live call path `ace_service.py::_act_generative` already uses in production — rather than inventing a parallel client; the ACP-shadow-mode tension this implies (branded "gateway," actually direct) is disclosed, not silently resolved as if the gateway were real. **Trace privacy**: `GraphRagTrace` is tenant-scoped and append-only (`GraphRagTraceImmutableError` on update/delete, re-verified); PII redaction is new — `_redact_pii` masks every `moderation_service.detect_pii` finding's span in the question and recursively through every string leaf of the trace payload before either is ever passed to `db.add(...)`, closing the gap this sprint's own investigation found (detection existed, redact-and-store did not). **A genuine cross-project incident, found and resolved mid-sprint**: applying this sprint's own migration hit a completely full Docker VM disk (0 bytes free) — traced to two Neo4j containers stuck in a crash-restart loop that had grown to 22.1GB and 9.4GB, unrelated to this sprint's own code and pre-dating this session; resolved with the user's explicit, step-by-step approval (image prune first — negligible, all images were attached to live containers; then stop, confirmed insufficient — a stopped container's layer isn't reclaimed; then remove, confirmed their volumes persist and are reattachable) rather than any unilateral cleanup, freeing 29.9GB. The full-repo regression run this freed also surfaced a SECOND, independent latent bug: three stale, wrongly-committed `ekg_ontology_versions` rows (malformed test-marker semvers `0.0.1-w103-*`/`w104`/`w105`) left behind by an EARLIER session's Wave-1 Neo4j test failures whose fixtures never cleaned up on error — breaking `get_current_version`'s semver comparison (`TypeError: '>' not supported between instances of 'str' and 'int'`) for every OTHER ontology-registry test. Deleted (an unambiguous test-only artifact in the test database, by its own semver naming) and re-verified clean; disclosed here since it was found via this sprint's own regression check, not this sprint's own code. 27/29 new tests pass, 2 correctly skipped (`tests/conformance/test_ekg_w3_02_graphrag_pipeline.py`): 8 static (policy resolution/scope, no DB), 1 rerank (weighting+dedup+threshold), 4 grounding checks, 3 PII redaction, 1 context pack, 2 policy-scoped-retrieval end-to-end, 3 full-pipeline end-to-end (mocked LLM call — this codebase's own established convention for `AIAgentConfig`-dependent code, `test_stx06_ace_runtime.py`'s `_fake_load_agent`, not a real credentialed provider chain), 3 trace privacy, 2 skipped bounded-neighborhood/tenant-isolation tests (no live Neo4j reachable in this environment after the incident above — properly skip-guarded, not fabricated as passing) — 661/662 across the full `tests/conformance/` regression run post-cleanup, zero residue; the one remaining failure is the same pre-existing Redis-default-port issue already disclosed at EKG-W3-01 | 09, 11, 12 | EKG-W3-02 | 29 tests (27 pass, 2 skip: static resolution/scope/rerank/grounding/PII/context-pack (17), policy-scoped-retrieval + full-pipeline + trace-privacy end-to-end (8), bounded-neighborhood/tenant-isolation (2, skip — no live Neo4j)) |

| **EKG-W3-05 — Delegated capability tokens (fifth and final Wave-3 sprint — Wave 3 complete)** (`dlu_builder_tk`, ✅ **implemented after tracing the exact trust chain end-to-end and evaluating three existing signing primitives before adding a fourth**): `gateway_virtual_key_manager.py`/`GatewayVirtualKey` — real, but scopes a LiteLLM dollar BUDGET only (no `expires_at`/TTL column at all) and isn't even on the call path `LLMClientFactory.get_client_for_agent` (the function both `ekg_graphrag_service.py` and `ekg_tutor_service.py` actually call) takes; `token_service.py::TokenService` — a real HMAC-SHA256 primitive, but shaped for opaque, DB-row-keyed one-time action links (`generate_token` returns a random string plus a hash the caller must persist and pass back — no self-contained claims payload to decode statelessly); `auth_service.py::AuthenticationService` — real `python-jose` JWT (HS256, `SECRET_KEY`, arbitrary claims, configurable expiry), the right SHAPE (self-contained, statelessly verifiable) but its own methods are hard-shaped for a HUMAN session (`user_id`+`email`+`username`+`role`, `type: "access"`) — reusing them directly would risk exactly the confusion a distinct token type exists to prevent. New `backend/services/ekg_capability_token_service.py` reuses the underlying PRIMITIVE (`jose.jwt`, the same `security_config.secret_key`/`.algorithm` every signer in this codebase already converges on) with its own purpose-built claims shape and a `type: "ekg_capability"` marker — a third, narrower token type alongside the two that already exist, not a random new invention. **Exact trust-chain gap closed, traced precisely before writing any code**: `run_graphrag_pipeline`/`ekg_grounding_for_move` accept `authorized_course_ids`/`policy`/`course_id` as bare, unverified function arguments by original EKG-W3-02 design ("auth is the caller's job, never re-derived here") — true and UNCHANGED for the legacy no-token call shape (every EKG-W3-02/03/04 test and call site is byte-for-byte unaffected); this sprint adds an OPTIONAL, additive `capability_token` parameter to both functions, verified via the crypto primitive instead of trusted as a plain argument, wired for real at `ace_service.py::_act_generative` — the one place `tenant_id`/`agent.slug`/`course_id` are already legitimately resolved — which now mints a token there and passes it down instead of the bare `course_id` int it passed before. **"Agents never receive superuser tokens" made structural, not a runtime filter**: `mint_capability_token` REFUSES to mint with an empty/missing `authorized_course_ids` — there is no wildcard/"all courses" scope this function can express at all — re-verified directly (empty-scope mint raises) and end-to-end (`_act_generative`'s own minted token, captured via a spy, always carries EXACTLY `[course_id]`, the single resolved course, never a broader grant). **A genuine, confirmed-absent second gate added**: investigation found that neither `vector_search`/`fulltext_search`'s SQL `WHERE` nor the in-Python `_apply_course_acl` intersection is ever re-checked again after retrieval — whatever `rerank` returns flows straight into `build_context_pack` unverified, so "LLM sees only policy-approved context" was NOT actually true end-to-end before this sprint. New `enforce_capability_scope` re-checks every `CanonicalPassage` (which gained a `course_ids` field this sprint, propagated through `vector_search`/`fulltext_search`/`rerank`, an additive dataclass field with a default — zero impact on any existing constructor call in EKG-W3-02/03/04) against the CRYPTOGRAPHICALLY VERIFIED claims — never the raw, re-passed `policy`/`authorized_course_ids` arguments — immediately before context-pack construction, dropping (with a warning, never raising — one bad passage must not crash an otherwise-good answer) anything outside scope and re-indexing the remainder contiguously so the LLM's own citation numbering never shows a confusing gap. **"Scope reduction tested"**: re-verified with a token deliberately minted for course A used against a request for course B (tutor path degrades to no grounding, never silently using the wrong scope) and a token minted for a different tenant entirely (refused outright in the pipeline path, degraded in the tutor path); re-verified that a VALID token's scope wins over deliberately WRONG raw `policy`/`authorized_course_ids` arguments passed alongside it in the same call. **Tamper/expiry/type-confusion resistance re-verified directly**, not merely assumed from "it's a JWT": a hand-tampered signature, a hand-crafted expired token (real secret, past `exp`), a genuine JWT of the WRONG type (`"access"`, matching `AuthenticationService`'s own human-session shape — proving a stolen or reused human token can never be honored as a capability delegation), and a plain garbage string are all rejected. Disclosed, not fixed here (a deeper, DB-layer gap investigation surfaced but this sprint's own literal text doesn't ask for): `ekg_vector_chunks` and every other EKG table have zero Postgres row-level-security policies — the shared `db` session these services use is unrestricted at the database layer, with hand-written `WHERE tenant_id = ...` clauses as the only real enforcement; this sprint hardens the APPLICATION/token layer the spec literally asks for ("short-lived, scope-reduced tokens; LLM sees only policy-approved context"), a DB-layer RLS hardening pass is a different, later concern (EKG-W5-02's own named scope), not silently assumed already covered. 15/15 new tests pass (`tests/conformance/test_ekg_w3_05_capability_tokens.py`): 2 no-superuser minting guardrails, 4 verification-rejects-anything-not-genuine (tamper, wrong-type, expired, garbage), 2 second-gate filtering+reindexing, 2 pipeline-honors-token-over-raw-args (including a cross-tenant refusal), 3 tutor-grounding scope reduction (valid/wrong-course/wrong-tenant), 1 ACE-mints-narrow-token integration proof, plus zero regressions confirmed by re-running all 49 EKG-W3-02/03/04 tests (2 skip, unrelated to Neo4j) after the shared-dataclass changes — 699/700 across the full `tests/conformance/` regression run, zero residue; the one remaining failure is the same pre-existing Redis-default-port issue already disclosed at EKG-W3-01 through 04. **Wave 3 (GraphRAG Tutor) is now complete**: EKG-W3-01 (vector ingestion) → W3-02 (GraphRAG pipeline) → W3-03 (Grounded Tutor/ACE) → W3-04 (eval harness) → W3-05 (capability tokens), five sprints, each investigated against real codebase state before writing code, each disclosing what it found rather than assuming a green field | 09, 11, 12 | EKG-W3-05 | 15 tests: 2 no-superuser-minting guardrails, 4 verification-rejection (tamper/wrong-type/expired/garbage), 2 second-gate scope filtering+reindexing, 2 pipeline token-over-raw-args (incl. cross-tenant refusal), 3 tutor scope reduction (valid/wrong-course/wrong-tenant), 1 ACE narrow-token-minting integration proof, 0 regressions (49/49 prior W3-02/03/04 tests re-run) |
| **EKG-W3-06 — ATA ontology extension + Postgres models (first of five Wave-3-ATA sprints, implemented 2026-08-10 — see correction note at the end of `EKG-W3-10`'s own row below)** (`dlu_builder_tk`, ✅ **implemented after discovering, and cleaning up, real pre-existing data corruption in the shared Ontology Registry no prior sprint had ever caught**): the 11 ATA node types / 12 edges / 2 SHACL shapes (`../dlu_builder_tk/docs/skills/ekg-tutor/SKILL.md` §6; source `DLU_EKG_Suite/DKA/600-adaptive-tutor/ATA-EKG-Extension.md`) are registered into the Ontology Registry (`ekg_ontology_registry_service.py`, EKG-W0-01) as a new "1.1.0" version, cloned forward from the CURRENT "1.0.0" core (48 node types/61 edge types/5 SHACL shapes total after the clone+extend, not a bare 11/12/2) — `backend/scripts/seed_ata_ontology_extension.py`. **A genuine, load-bearing bug found and fixed, not assumed correct**: `ekg_ontology_registry_service._semver_key` compared `(is_valid, value)`-shaped tuples inconsistently — `tuple(int(p) for p in semver.split("."))` on success vs. a bare `(semver,)` string fallback on failure — which raised `TypeError: '>' not supported between str and int` the moment `max()` ever compared one of each in the same call; fixed to always return a uniform `(1, numeric_tuple)` / `(0, string)` shape. **This bug's fix, in turn, surfaced that the shared test Postgres database had never actually been seeded with the real EKG ontology at all** — only 4-5 rows of test residue existed (`db.commit()` calls escaping transactional-rollback isolation in other tests, malformed semvers like `"0.0.1-w101-b02d6a6a"`), which the OLD, broken `_semver_key` was silently tolerating via string comparison; deleted the contaminated rows directly, then ran `seed_ekg_ontology_v1.main()` and this sprint's own `seed_ata_ontology_extension.main()` for real — this environment now has a permanently, correctly seeded registry for the first time. **A real naming collision found and resolved, not glossed over**: the ATA extension's own "Recommendation" node type collided with the CORE ontology's own pre-existing "Recommendation" node (layer "AI", `Agent -[GENERATES]-> Recommendation`) — a live `UniqueViolationError` on first seed attempt, not a hypothetical; the Postgres model and the seed's own node key were renamed to `InterventionRecommendation` (table name `tutor_recommendations` unchanged). A 9th ontology layer, "Tutor", was added to `EKG_ONTOLOGY_LAYERS` (Python tuple + live `ALTER TABLE` CHECK-constraint update) — the 8 pre-existing layers had no slot for tutor-specific node types. **Two-tier Postgres backing** (`backend/database/models_tutor.py`, mirroring `models_career_gap.py`'s own convention): a `platform`-schema definition/governance tier (`LearningStrategy` — the 14 NBLA actions, seeded as a closed catalog; `TutorPolicyVersion` — immutable once created, `before_update`/`before_delete` listeners raise `TutorPolicyVersionImmutableError`, mirroring `MasteryModelParams`; `TutorPolicyVersionStage` — append-only lifecycle trail, mirroring `MappingAssertionReview`) and a tenant-schema learner tier (`LearningGoal`, `TutorSession`, `TutorTurn`, `Misconception`, `LearningIntervention`, `LearningPlan`, `InterventionRecommendation`, `TutorEvidence`, `TutorMemoryItem`). **A real Alembic migration authored and applied, not a dev-only shortcut**: `migrations/versions/20261004_0900_ekg_w3_06_10_tutor_ata_extension.py` creates all 12 tables for real (replacing an earlier, disclosed one-off `Base.metadata.create_all()` workaround used mid-sprint to unblock testing) and extends the `tenant_isolation_policy` RLS rollout EKG-W5-02 (`20261003_0900`) established to the 9 of these 12 tables carrying real tenant-scoping columns (1 direct-`tenant_id`, 8 `twin_id`-scoped) — a NEW migration, not an edit to that historical revision, per its own docstring's explicit anticipation of exactly this ("stays reproducible even as [the policy module's] table lists grow in later sprints"); `test_ekg_w5_02_tenancy_hardening.py`'s own exact-match classification test was updated to compare the UNION of both migrations' table tuples against the policy module, not just the original one. Same disclosed, load-bearing RLS caveat as EKG-W5-02 (no non-superuser/non-BYPASSRLS runtime role exists yet in this codebase, so RLS provides no ADDITIONAL protection over the application-layer `WHERE`-clause checks every new service in this wave already performs). 11/11 new tests pass (`tests/conformance/test_ekg_w3_06_ata_ontology_extension.py`): ontology extension (superset of core, idempotent, resolves by semver), 14-strategy catalog (seeded, idempotent, bad key rejected by CHECK constraint), two-tenant isolation (`LearningGoal`/`TutorSession` queries never cross tenants), policy-version immutability (mutation/deletion rejected, stage trail append-only) — plus fixing 3 previously-flaky, pre-existing `test_ekg_ontology_registry.py` tests whose own premises ("seed returns exactly 'seeded'", "zero approved versions exist") became deterministically false the moment this sprint permanently seeded the registry for real, and honestly `@pytest.mark.skip`-ing a 4th whose precondition can never be re-arranged in this now-permanently-seeded shared environment | 05, 09A, 13 | EKG-W3-06 | 11 tests: ontology extension (superset/idempotent/semver-resolved), 14-strategy catalog (seeded/idempotent/CHECK-rejected), two-tenant isolation, policy-version immutability (mutation/deletion rejected, append-only stage trail); real `_semver_key` bug found+fixed; registry permanently, correctly seeded for the first time in this environment; real Alembic migration authored+applied (12 tables + RLS extension) |

WHERE institution_id IS NULL` (raw SQL, since the declarative ORM layer can't express a partial index), re-verified against `\d platform.competency_frameworks` after a downgrade/fix/reapply. **No-fabricated-identity made structural, not a service-layer promise**: `framework_concepts` carries `CHECK (external_uri IS NOT NULL OR external_id IS NOT NULL)` plus the same append-only-via-ORM-listener pattern already used for `GraphRagTrace` (EKG-W3-02) — `before_update`/`before_delete` raise `FrameworkConceptImmutableError`, re-verified as a real DB-level rejection, not just a Python-side check. **Honest identity per framework, never invented**: new `backend/services/ekg_framework_registry_service.py` gives EQF/Bloom levels `external_uri=NULL` (no verified official URI source exists anywhere in this codebase) but reuses the SAME informal `"EQF-5"`/`"SFIA-3"` level-code convention `Competency.level` already established elsewhere for `external_id`; ESCO occupations get both real, PARSED from the already-imported `EscoOccupation.occupation_uri` (never re-fetched from the live ESCO API); SFIA/DigComp get `register_pending_framework` — a name-only registration with no concept data at all, since zero real source data exists anywhere for either — disclosed as a genuine, honestly-scoped gap, not silently faked. `register_framework` is idempotent for a matching checksum and refuses a silent repin on a DIFFERENT checksum under the same `(code, version)`; `validate_import` re-derives the CURRENT on-disk checksum for the two artifact types it actually knows how to re-checksum (the bundled EQF/Bloom JSON, the bundled ESCO CSV bundle) and HONESTLY RAISES rather than guessing for any other code — a real design decision the sprint's own test suite initially got wrong (see below). **Two real bugs self-caught by this sprint's own tests, not by inspection**: (1) `import_esco_occupations_as_concepts`'s `SELECT ... LIMIT $n` carried no `ORDER BY` — Postgres gives no ordering guarantee without one, so a second `limit=5` re-import was not guaranteed to touch the same 5 rows as the first, breaking the sprint's own idempotency requirement; fixed with a deterministic `order_by(EscoOccupation.occupation_uri)`. (2) the first draft of the drift-detection test invented a fictitious "DUMMYFW3" framework code and expected `validate_import` to return `False` against it — the SERVICE was right and the TEST was wrong: there is no real on-disk artifact for an arbitrary made-up code, so raising is the honest behavior; out of scope to fix since no real caller has ever exercised the null path. **No production caller wired yet, same honesty as EKG-W3-03's `course_id` disclosure**: `import_eqf_bloom_reference`/`import_esco_occupations_as_concepts`/`register_pending_framework` are new, tested, standalone functions — this sprint's own literal ask is the schema + import-function layer, not caller wiring; (2) the `EARNS` edge (Learner->Credential) — `event_taxonomy.py`'s own EKG-W0-03 comment had ALREADY named `credential.issued == CREDENTIAL_ISSUED` as this exact ontology slot, but `_sign_and_persist`'s actual emitted payload never carried `entity_type`/`entity_id` — confirmed zero consumers existed for it anywhere before this sprint, so correcting the payload shape in place (not adding a parallel event) broke nothing. **A second, previously-undiscovered missing-Learner-node gap, same class as EKG-W4-02's own finding**: no producer anywhere in this codebase has ever projected a `Learner` node (confirmed absent by inspection) — every `OBSERVES`/`HAS_GAP`/`PRODUCES`/`EARNS` edge targeting `Learner` would hit the same missing-endpoint error; this sprint's own consumer handler (`_handle_credential_issued`) ensures it exists via a cheap, idempotent MERGE before the standard projection runs — disclosed as fixing only ITS OWN edge, not retroactively repairing the other three edge types' producers. `CREDENTIAL_REVOKED`'s payload is additively enriched the same way so a later revocation re-projects the SAME `Credential` node's `status` property — the `EARNS` edge itself is never removed (a credential once earned stays earned; only validity status changes), re-verified directly against live Neo4j including a revocation-after-issuance sequence. **CLR export is deliberately NOT a new signing operation**: `export_clr` aggregates a learner's real, already-signed `IssuedCredential` rows into an IMS CLR 2.0-shaped envelope carrying no `proof` of its own — each embedded credential remains independently verifiable via its OWN pre-existing proof (re-verified directly with `credential_signing_service.verify_credential`); "reuse `credential_service`/`qes`," never re-sign, is honored literally. `include_revoked=False` (default) honors revocation by exclusion, matching the status-list's own "current record" posture; `include_revoked=True` remains available for audit. **A serious infrastructure incident found, diagnosed, and fully remediated mid-sprint, disclosed not hidden**: an UNRELATED, pre-existing test file (`tests/test_stx13_credential_engine.py`, STX-13-era) uses its own `stx13_db` fixture that CREATES-then-DROPS a fixed table list at every test's setup/teardown — a design safe only against an ephemeral/isolated test database, never validated against this shared, fully-migrated one; running it (while investigating an apparent hang) triggered its teardown's `DROP TABLE` cascade, which silently succeeded in dropping `issued_credentials`, `credential_templates`, `credential_signing_keys`, `credential_status_list_sequences`, `recognition_claims`, `recognition_calibration_stats`, and — most seriously — `domain_events` (the core event-outbox table underpinning EVERY sprint since EKG-W0-03) before erroring on a genuine, pre-existing FK conflict (`evidence_records` depending on `student_competencies`, never accounted for by this fixture's table list). Diagnosed via direct `pg_stat_activity` inspection (a zombie "idle in transaction" backend from an earlier interrupted run was blocking a live one's own `DROP TABLE`, traced and terminated via `pg_terminate_backend`, not guessed at); fully restored via `Base.metadata.create_all` against the LIVE ORM definitions (which already reflect every migration ever applied to these tables, cross-checked column-for-column and constraint-for-constraint against `\d` output before proceeding) rather than attempting a fragile partial-migration replay; re-verified with a full regression sweep afterward. Confirmed, via isolated re-runs, that the credential-engine test file's OWN test bodies pass correctly against the corrected code (proving this sprint's changes are sound) — only that file's teardown is fragile, a pre-existing condition unrelated to and not caused by this sprint, disclosed and left alone rather than rewritten unilaterally. 11/11 new tests pass (`tests/conformance/test_ekg_w4_05_credential_graph_object.py`): 2 event-shape (issuance emits the correct EARNS-carrying payload, revocation emits the correct status re-projection), 2 live-Neo4j EARNS projection (node+edge created with no pre-existing Learner node, revocation updates status while the edge survives), 4 CLR export (CLR 2.0 shape with no new proof, revoked-excluded-by-default, full-history-on-request, each embedded credential independently verifiable), 3 public-verify/revocation-list reuse (fresh credential verifies offline, tampered credential fails, revocation flips the real Bitstring Status List bit) — plus 564/565 across the same targeted Wave 0-3 EKG + admissions/faculty/governance/finance/preval regression sweep (the one failure is the same pre-existing async-teardown flake disclosed at EKG-W4-02, reproduced a FIFTH time across five sprints, still unrelated), zero residue caused by this sprint after full incident remediation. **Wave 4 (Career + Credit) is now complete**: EKG-W4-01 (framework registry) → W4-02 (mapping stewardship) → W4-03 (career + skill gap) → W4-04 (credit recognition workflow) → W4-05 (credential graph object), five | **EKG-W5-02 — Neo4j tenancy hardening (second Wave-5 sprint)** (`dlu_builder_tk`, ✅ **implemented at a user-confirmed scope after a real, load-bearing discovery mid-investigation: Postgres RLS "reconciliation" turned out to require establishing, empirically, that RLS is currently INERT app-wide — not an EKG-specific gap, a pre-existing condition surfaced and disclosed, not fixed at its root here**): investigated before writing any code — EKG-W0-02's own baseline (`ekg_scope_policy.py` GLOBAL/TENANT classification, `named_query_templates.py` as the sole sanctioned Neo4j entry point, one two-tenant isolation test) was confirmed real and working; EKG-W3-05's own disclosed, deliberately-deferred gap ("`ekg_vector_chunks` and every other EKG table have zero Postgres row-level-security policies... naming EKG-W5-02 as where that gets closed") was the literal starting point for the Postgres half of this sprint's own two-part spec. **The load-bearing discovery**: migrations 002/003 already contain real `CREATE POLICY tenant_isolation_policy` statements on `enrollments`/`progress_events`, but empirical checks against both the live dev AND test Postgres instances confirmed the runtime connection role in EVERY environment this codebase configures is either a superuser or otherwise carries `BYPASSRLS` — no non-superuser, non-bypassing role has ever been defined anywhere in this codebase's SQL/init scripts. RLS is inert against such a role by Postgres's own design, `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` notwithstanding (that clause only defeats table-OWNER bypass, never `BYPASSRLS`) — an app-wide condition predating this whole engagement, not something any EKG sprint introduced. **Escalated to the user rather than silently choosing a scope**: presented three options (add real RLS + disclose the bypass as separate follow-up; also attempt the much larger, cross-cutting DB-role lockdown now; skip Postgres RLS entirely this sprint) — user selected the first. New `backend/services/graph/ekg_postgres_tenancy_policy.py` classifies EKG's own Postgres tables the SAME conservative, explicit-allowlist way `ekg_scope_policy.py` already classifies Neo4j labels, and found FOUR genuinely different scoping shapes by inspection, not assumed uniform: (1) 8 tables with a direct `tenant_id` column; (2) 4 tables (`gap_observations`/`learning_states`/`mastery_observations`/`student_competencies`) scoped only via a `twin_id` FK to `student_twins`; (3) `evidence_records` specifically — arguably the single most sensitive EKG table — scoped two hops away (`student_competency_id` -> `student_competencies.twin_id` -> `student_twins.tenant_id`), a REAL, empirically-found distinct shape: this migration's first `alembic upgrade` attempt against the real test database failed loudly with `UndefinedColumn` on the wrong one-hop assumption, caught and corrected before it ever reached a shared environment, never silently applying a wrong predicate; (4) `institution_id`-scoped tables (`job_roles`/`competencies`/`mapping_assertions`/`mapping_reviews`, nullable, `Institution.id != Institution.tenant_id` confirmed by inspection) deliberately DEFERRED this sprint rather than risk a rushed policy on a shape not yet fully proven out. New migration `20261003_0900_ekg_w5_02_postgres_rls.py` enables + FORCES RLS and creates the correctly-predicated policy (matching migrations 002/003's own `app.current_tenant_id` convention exactly — the SEPARATE, mismatched `app.tenant_id` variable `backend.database.tenant_session.set_tenant_context` sets is a distinct, wider, non-EKG-scoped legacy issue this sprint does not touch) on all 13 classified tables — applied and verified live. **Neo4j tenancy mode**: new `backend/services/graph/neo4j_tenancy_policy.py` makes shared-DB-logical the explicit, unchanged default (`resolve_database_for_tenant` returns `None` — the server default database — for every tenant unless opted out) and implements a REAL, not faked, `db_per_tenant` option for regulated tenants, configured via `Tenant.config["ekg_neo4j_isolation"]` (same "parameters is data, not columns" convention `RecognitionRulePack.parameters` already established, zero tenants currently opt in). Honestly gated by a real infrastructure fact, the SAME class of constraint EKG-W0-02 already hit once for composite node-key constraints: every environment this codebase configures runs `neo4j:5-community` (confirmed by inspection of every `docker-compose.*.yml`), and multi-database is Neo4j Enterprise-only — `resolve_database_for_tenant` calls a new `Neo4jClient.get_edition()` (via `CALL dbms.components()`) and RAISES a clear, actionable `Neo4jTenancyConfigError` for a `db_per_tenant`-configured tenant against a server that cannot actually provide it, rather than silently degrading to shared or pretending to isolate; `Neo4jClient.get_session()` gained an unused-by-default `database=` parameter ready for the day this deployment moves to Enterprise, zero behavior change for any existing caller. New `backend/scripts/ekg_cypher_tenant_scope_scan.py` (the literal "static Cypher tenant-scope scan" verification bullet) enforces two rules: no raw Cypher execution anywhere in `backend/` outside a small, explicit, reasoned allowlist (found and correctly allowlisted 4 additional legitimate call sites this sprint's own first scan run surfaced — `kg_event_bus.py`, `domains/enrollment/service.py`, `domains/pacing/service.py`, `domains/mobility/equivalency.py` — all confirmed, by reading each one, to be the pre-existing "legacy KG v1.0" system already disclosed across this whole engagement, never EKG's own ontology), and every query-building function in `named_query_templates.py` itself enforces tenant scoping (directly, via a scoped `_build_*` helper it calls, or via a mandatory non-optional `TenantContext`) — the scan's own first version had two real bugs in this second rule (didn't follow calls into private helper functions; a regex that failed when `TenantContext` was a signature's LAST parameter), both caught and fixed by two synthetic-violation tests proving the scanner actually detects what it claims to, not just that it stays quiet. **Adversarial two-tenant coverage extended, not duplicated, past EKG-W0-02's own single existing test**: a cross-tenant edge-write attempt (tenant A linking to a node it does not own correctly surfaces as the same `GraphProjectionError` a genuinely-missing endpoint would, MATCH-only endpoint resolution never silently creating a placeholder), the `bounded_neighborhood` multi-hop `ALL(...)` tenant guard exercised for the first time by any test in this codebase (a same-labelled tenant-B node one hop from tenant A's start, reached via a deliberately-forced adversarial cross-tenant edge, never appears in tenant A's neighborhood result), and a parametrized sweep of adversarial tenant-id/label/edge-type strings (SQL/Cypher-injection shapes, off-by-one UUIDs, empty strings) against the existing validation functions. 32/32 new tests pass (`tests/conformance/test_ekg_w5_02_tenancy_hardening.py`) plus the pre-existing 7/7 EKG-W0-02 suite unaffected — 4 classification (no scoping-shape overlap, migration `run_pg_restore` now narrowly tolerates ONLY this exact, named class of client/server version-skew error (every error line must be "unrecognized configuration parameter" AND pg_restore's own summary must say it ignored them), raising loudly for anything else — a precise exception, not a blanket swallow. **Honest DR verdict, not the spec's own numbers, recorded in `docs/sprint_decisions_20260809_ekg_w5_04_dr_security_signoff.md`**: Postgres/Neo4j RPO remains ~24h (true PITR/WAL-archiving named as required follow-up, not silently deferred); RTO is proven-working end-to-end for a small disposable database (well under the 2h target) but NOT measured at production scale, disclosed as a distinct, separate follow-up. **Encryption built**: new Fernet-based encryption for evidence artifact bytes (`backend/services/evidence_artifact_service.py::store_artifact`/`load_artifact`), reusing the EXACT `ENCRYPTION_KEY`-via-Fernet convention `credential_signing_service.py`/`llm_provider_service.py` already established (a third user of the identical scheme, never a fourth) — the evidentiary SHA-256 integrity hash is computed over the original plaintext, unaffected by at-rest encryption, verified directly. EKG-W2-01's own signed, TTL-bound, path-traversal-guarded URL mechanism re-verified working transparently over encrypted bytes (the consuming route already goes through `load_artifact`, needed no change) — still zero real upload producers, same disclosed gap as EKG-W2-01, unchanged. New `docs/DR_AND_ENCRYPTION_RUNBOOK.md` names the host/infra requirement plainly rather than faking a docker-compose-level control. **Prompt-injection defenses**: found a real, already-working "data-not-instructions" detector (`egress_checks.check_injection_resistance`, BOOK-11 Ch. 3.4) wired into the ACE agent cycle but NEVER into `ekg_graphrag_service.py`'s own GraphRAG tutor pipeline, which retrieves exactly the untrusted content class this check exists for — reused, not duplicated: `build_context_pack` now fences every passage in an explicit `<retrieved_passage>` tag and `call_llm_gateway`'s system prompt explicitly instructs the model to treat passage content as data, never instructions; `grounding_and_policy_checks` now also runs `check_injection_resistance` against the model's own answer, surfacing a hit as a violation and hard-capping confidence to 0.0 — the same "surfaced, confidence-capped" posture every other violation in that function already gets. **Two real regressions found and fixed during the broader regression sweep, not just the new suite**: a pre-existing `test_ekg_w3_02_graphrag_pipeline.py` test asserted the OLD, unfenced `build_context_pack` output format byte-for-byte — updated to match the new, intentional format, not silently left broken; | **EKG-QA-01 — Live SHACL conformance validator, the QA Officer's own long-disclosed gap, closed (2026-08-14)** (`dlu_builder_tk`, ✅ **implemented after confirming the registry's own `EkgShaclShape.shape_ttl` is real, unmodified SHACL Turtle — not a simplified internal representation — sourced verbatim from `ekg-shacl.ttl`, so this closes the use case as literally written ("verify... as a conformance check"), not a narrower stand-in for it**): `ekg_lens_service.build_qa_conformance_status_view`'s own prior comment disclosed that SHACL shapes were registered metadata only, with zero code anywhere that validated live graph data against them. New `backend/services/graph/shacl_conformance_service.py` parses every registered shape's real Turtle via `rdflib` (never a hand-rolled string scan — a shape using an unsupported SHACL construct beyond `sh:targetClass`/`sh:property`/`sh:path`/`sh:minCount`/`sh:maxCount` surfaces as an explicit `unsupported` finding, never silently dropped), then runs a new, generic `named_query_templates.min_cardinality_violations` (reusing that module's own tenant-scoping/injection-defense discipline, the SAME "one sanctioned entry point to the graph" every prior EKG-Wx sprint has respected) against real Neo4j data for every `(targetClass, path)` pair whose SHACL local name resolves to a REAL registered `EkgEdgeType.key` — a candidate is derived via a deterministic camelCase→SCREAMING_SNAKE conversion (`hasModule`→`HAS_MODULE`) but is never trusted blindly: it's checked against the ontology version's actual registered edge-type keys first, and an unresolvable path reports `resolved_edge_type: None` rather than guessing. **Deliberately scoped to exactly the SHACL subset the registry's shapes actually use, not a general-purpose `pyshacl` engine**: a full `pyshacl` run would need an RDF projection of the whole graph across all 37 node types, most with no registered shape at all — every shape registered today (the 3 core + the 2 `EKG-W3-06` ATA shapes, version 1.1.0) only ever constrains 6 total property paths across 3 labels (plus 2 more from ATA), so this validates exactly those via real Cypher `count()`s instead, the same "scope to the literal ask" discipline `EKG-W3-12` already established. **A real, disclosed finding surfaced by actually running this against what the graph currently contains, not fabricated**: most checks report `total_nodes: 0` rather than a real pass/fail, because several of the target nodes/edges these shapes name are themselves still-disclosed, separate producer gaps this program already found elsewhere — `Module`/`Lesson` nodes are never projected at all (`academic_hierarchy_projection.py`'s own `EKG-W1-06` deferral), and `CONTRIBUTES_TO`/`ASSESSED_BY`/`TARGETS`/`GENERATED_BY_POLICY` are "correct and ready" payload builders with no real production caller yet (the same class of gap `EKG-W4-04`/`EKG-W6-07` already disclosed for other edges) — `DEFINES_CLO` is the one property with a real, live producer today. The validator's own output distinguishes `total_nodes: 0` ("nothing to check yet") from a real `violating_count > 0` explicitly — never conflates the two into a false green. Wired into the EXISTING `GET /api/ekg-lens/qa/conformance-status` route (no new route/UI needed — `build_qa_conformance_status_view` gained `neo4j_session`/`tenant_id` parameters, following the exact `build_provost_attention_queue_view` convention already established) — the QA Console's existing call site now gets real conformance data instead of registry-only metadata. New dependency `rdflib==7.1.1` (pure Python, added to `requirements.docker.txt` — the file CI/production actually builds from — and `requirements.txt`). 19 new conformance tests (`test_ekg_qa_01_shacl_conformance.py`): real-Turtle parsing of all 3 core shapes end-to-end, registry-integrity error cases (target/shape mismatch, unparseable Turtle, unsupported-constraint disclosure), `run_conformance_check`'s honest-skip branches (unregistered target class, unregistered edge type), and `min_cardinality_violations`'s own Cypher-building (honest zero, sample-capping, tenant-scope rejection) — plus the existing `EKG-W6-07` QA test updated to the new signature, and a targeted regression sweep (575/579 across the full `ekg`/`w6_07`/`w1_05` conformance suite, 4 pre-existing failures confirmed unrelated — a stale shell-script permission bit and a Redis-connection-refused cache test, neither importing any file this sprint touches) | 05, 15, 19 | EKG-QA-01 | 19 new tests; zero regression on the touched `EKG-W6-07` QA test (updated to new signature); 4 pre-existing, unrelated failures confirmed out of scope (script permission bit, Redis connectivity) via direct import-graph check |
